# Gate proofs

A gate counts only once it has been made to go red by reintroducing the defect it claims to catch. This file records the mutation, the exact failure it produced, and the bound the gate carries in its own header. Newest first.

Auditing a gate means reaching what was measured at the time, never the sentence the gate carries about itself: a gate and its claim can be wrong together and look exactly like a gate that is right.

Every entry names the tree its numbers were taken on. Eight name none: the foundation batch that `7c6f905` added — *sweep3d* through *check: the authored chapter is complete and consistent* — was taken on the uncommitted foundation tree of 2026-09-08, before the first commit, so those name files rather than revisions; the first commit carries the same files.

## Read one entry, not the file

This file is long and gets longer with every gate, and it is **history rather than a rule**. What binds you is the claim in the gate's own header, and `AGENTS.md`'s Gates section lists every gate with its bound. Come here for one of three reasons and read only the entry the index sends you to:

- **You are changing a gate.** Read that gate's entries — *Claim* and *What it does not prove* — so the change does not quietly retire the proof. Behaviour that moves past what the entry measured needs a new proof in the same commit.
- **You are writing a proof.** Copy the shape below. Reading a neighbouring entry for the form costs forty times as much and gets you the same six headings.
- **You are auditing a claim.** A gate and the sentence in its header can be wrong together and look exactly like a gate that is right, so the question is always what was measured, never what the header says.

### The shape of an entry

Newest at the top. One `##` per claim, headed `<gate>: <the claim in one line> (<the file that holds it>)`. Inside, in this order:

- **The tree.** The revision, plus any uncommitted work in it the numbers depend on. A number with no tree is not a measurement.
- **Claim**, quoted from the gate's own header, with the **bound** that header states — the page list, the seed set, the window, the viewport, the include list. A gate proves nothing past its bound.
- **Why it was needed**, when the gate came from a real defect: what shipped, and for how long.
- **The mutation, and the exact failure text it produced**, verbatim. A failure written from what the gate *would* say is not a proof. Prefer the real defect on the real file; where only a fixture can reach it, say so.
- **The restoration.** sha256 of every mutated file before and after, or an empty `git diff`. A proof that leaves the tree changed is a defect.
- **What it does not prove.** The half that gets read most: it is the standing list of what is still unwatched.

An arm that stays green is a result and is recorded as one — *shot: a Simplified page's figure word* records half a claim that no page in this repository can exercise, and that is the entry's finding.

## Index

Grouped by the file the gate lives in, because that is how they are looked up. Several gates carry more than one entry; where two say the same thing in different words, the line says so.

**`tools/shot.js`** — page loads, fonts, colour marks, figure geometry.

- [every page loads clean and lays out](#shot-every-page-loads-clean-and-lays-out-toolsshotjs) — the base claim: handshake reached, no console error, no failed request, no sideways scroll.
- [no character is drawn by a face the page never loaded](#shot-no-character-is-drawn-by-a-face-the-page-never-loaded-toolsshotjs-the-font-census) — the font census. Reads the DOM as it stands, so it presses nothing.
- [a figure's arithmetic reaching the DOM as a negative or as NaN](#shot-a-figures-arithmetic-reaching-the-dom-as-a-negative-or-as-nan-toolsshotjs-auditfiguregeometry) — `auditFigureGeometry`, after `<rect width="-0.1">` shipped to the live site.
- [no colour hook paints nothing, and no marked target is left without one](#shot-no-colour-hook-paints-nothing-and-no-marked-target-is-left-without-one-toolsshotjs) · [a page may not carry fewer colour hooks than its census says](#shot-a-page-may-not-carry-fewer-colour-hooks-than-its-census-says-toolsshotjs-hook_census--auditcolourhooks) · [the colour-hook check measures the channels its header says it measures](#shot-the-colour-hook-check-measures-the-channels-its-header-says-it-measures-toolsshotjs-auditcolourhooks) — **three holes in one check, found one after another.** Read together: presence, then a floor per page, then which CSS channels the check actually reads. The floor entry also carries the rule that a floor set earlier passes the partial loss it was written for.

The refusal is the finding.

- [every marked run on a second-book chapter page is visibly marked, on the rendered page](#flow--mark-audit-every-marked-run-on-a-second-book-chapter-page-is-visibly-marked-on-the-rendered-page-toolsflowjs-mark-audit) — reads the stylesheet's own rules and fails a run the page leaves unmarked; proved red by making the page say marked while showing no mark. **It cannot see a mark that repaints a deliberately coloured container**, and it does not visit the key page.
- [the two standing records' indexes agree with their entries, both directions](#standing-docs-the-two-standing-records-indexes-agree-with-their-entries-both-directions-teststanding-docstestjs) — a new gate, and on its first run it found a three-commit-old entry with no index line. **Its own first proof attempt was a silent no-op** from the same `[...]` trap that dropped the line it exists for.
- [the gate chain can run in a linked worktree](#control-chars-the-gate-chain-can-run-in-a-linked-worktree-and-the-ignore-list-still-excludes-something-testcontrol-charstestjs) — in a worktree `.git` is a file, so the suite could not pass outside the shared tree at all; the ignore list's anti-vacuity rule is kept and proved red.
- [a quotation of 通鑑 the page does not mark is invisible](#provenance-a-quotation-of-通鑑-the-page-does-not-mark-is-invisible-and-this-is-what-makes-it-visible-testprovenancetestjs) — the recorded half of *"make it perfectly clear what came from the book"*, with the twenty-one runs that were printed as the book's own sentences. **Four holes were found in it after it was written**: one by its own mutation proof (the key check matched `zj-key-removed`, because `-` is a word boundary) and three by a read-only review — a scanner that read only single-quoted literals, a module list that shrank when a file was renamed, and a page mark that was true to the corpus and false to the 異文 note beside it. All four are closed and proved.
- Bound, quoted from the header and restated here because it is the part a later reader will want: the page half's predicate is **the book's own 「」 brackets**, so a 通鑑 quotation set bare in the prose, or bracketed with 『』, is not seen; *"A one-character quotation"* is outside the check, because a single Han character in 「」 is far more often the book naming a word it is about (「命」, 「恒」, 「版」) than quoting 通鑑; a quotation with one character changed or re-punctuated until it is no longer a substring stops being 通鑑 and this file says nothing about it; **a textual note is exempt**, because an `<aside data-note="textual">` discusses the text itself and a run inside one may be a reading the received text does not carry (「不可」 is absent from the 底本 and the note says so) — so a quotation in one of those notes is held by `test/lexicon.test.js`, the weaker check; **whether the mark RENDERS as something a reader notices** is not proved here — the file reads markup and data, not pixels, and the run's frames are the evidence for that half; and a quotation of another work is not gated at all, because 史記, 戰國策, 胡三省注 and 韋昭注 are named in the sentence that quotes them, which is a fact about prose rather than an attribute a checker can read. On the figure side the predicate is narrower on purpose — identity with a **whole 句** — because a figure's fields are short labels as well as sentences: `courtNote: '天子之命'` is a label the figure wrote that happens to occur inside a 句 of the 禮論, and a substring predicate failed on it. A figure field holding a **fragment** of a 句 is therefore not seen, and a fragment in the book's prose is, which is where a fragment is quoted.
- [the study system's arithmetic](#scheduler-and-store-the-study-systems-arithmetic-testschedulertestjs-teststoretestjs) — scheduler and store, 42 tests.
- [the flags that keep chromium's crash dialog off the desktop](#browser-quiet-the-flags-that-keep-chromiums-crash-dialog-off-the-desktop-testbrowser-quiettestjs) — and see the defect register, 2026-09-16, for the round where this test was green while the preload never ran.

`# Six gates that were shipped unproved (2026-09-16)` below is a batch heading, not a claim: it marks six proofs written after the fact for gates that had already reached main.

## drive: at 90 °C the bilayer tank says Dispersed at every one of sixty reads across 30 s of its own clock (`tools/drive.js`, `bilayer hot-enough-and-nothing-assembles`)

- **The tree.** Branch `ch04-figs`. The recipe as committed at `7be7e66`; the red arms with `src/figures/bilayer.js` replaced by the file as it was at `f7cb330`, before this round; the green arm on `47f7497` and again on `236778d`, the branch with main and the other two chapter-4 branches merged in.
- **Claim**, from the step's own comment: *"The claim is about a WINDOW of the tank's own clock, so it is asserted across one: thirty seconds of clock walked forward with Step, half a second a press, and the verdict read after every press."* Step is real input and moves the clock by exactly half a second, so every run reads the same sixty clock values. **Bound**, from the same comment: one seed (whichever Reset gives at that point of the recipe), the lab's tank shape, 90 °C, sixty instants half a second apart; nothing at the temperatures between, nothing about a verdict that holds for less than half a second between two reads, nothing about any other tank shape.
- **Why it was needed.** GitHub's runner failed the step as it was: `micelle at t=8.033, 0.641 buried`. It pressed Release, slept 500 ms at a time until the clock passed 8 s, and asserted once, at an instant the machine's load chose. The figure reported one sweep's classification, and measured in Node on the figure's own model code (30 s of clock, a read every 0.05 s, the lab's 13.8 nm tank), one sweep at 90 °C was a sheet or micelles in 8.8-12.8% of reads over 23 seeds, its buried fraction 0.37-0.75 across the classifier's 0.62 line; the held verdict was dispersed at every read of all 23. The verdict is now what the tank has held over the last second (`SAMPLE_SWEEPS` in `src/figures/bilayer.js`).
- **The mutation and the failure.** `git show f7cb330:src/figures/bilayer.js > src/figures/bilayer.js`, the one-sweep verdict. `DRIVE_KINDS=bilayer node tools/drive.js`, exit 1, twice. With the Curl step as it was: `FAIL bilayer hot-enough-and-nothing-assembles: at 90 °C the jostling should win at every point of 30 s of clock: bilayer at t=2, 0.63 buried, after 4 reads`. With the Curl step as it is now, whose fresh tanks move the seed this step reads on: `FAIL bilayer hot-enough-and-nothing-assembles: at 90 °C the jostling should win at every point of 30 s of clock: micelle at t=3, 0.629 buried, after 6 reads`.
- **The restoration.** `git checkout src/figures/bilayer.js` after each arm, `git status --short` empty. Green: `drive: 7 steps over 1 figures passed`, exit 0, on `47f7497`.
- **What it does not prove — demonstrated, not asserted.** With the tank's ratio floor put back from 0.66 to 0.52 (`MIN_RATIO`), the floor that let a chapter page at 1024 px draw a 10.4 nm tank so crowded that 90 °C held a sheet or micelles in 89% of sweeps, this step passes all seven steps, exit 0: the lab's pane never reaches the floor. That is why [the flattest tank is held in a unit test](#unit-at-90-c-the-bilayer-tank-stays-dispersed-at-the-flattest-shape-the-figure-draws-testbilayer-modeltestjs). Nor does it prove anything about the four other molecules, or at 85-89 °C, where the sheet is melting and the verdict is allowed to change.

## drive: after Curl the bilayer reading says what Curl did, and stops calling closed rims open (`tools/drive.js`, `bilayer curl-takes-the-wrap-away-and-the-sheet-rolls-up`)

- **The tree.** The step as it stands is `034d406`, after an independent review of `f7cb330..a673ab6` found that it re-derived the figure's decision with a threshold of its own (2 nm where the figure decides at 1.0). It now reads the figure's own finding, `describe().curl`, and asserts that the edge rose when the figure says it opened. Red arms on `034d406` and, before the review, on `236778d`; green on `034d406`, `236778d` and `47f7497`.
- **Claim**, from the step's own comment: after Curl *"the reading speaks of what Curl did, not of the needle before it, and once the rims have closed it stops calling them open"*; and when Curl opens no rims, the overlay says so. The step walks the clock with Step from a fresh tank, up to four of them, until Curl opens rims. **Bound**: the wide layout at 1000x640 only, the words matched by regular expression (`two open ends`, `last hole closed`, `sealed both ends`, `capped them`, `no ends opened`), and at most four seeds.
- **Why it was needed.** Two defects, one in the figure and one in the gate. After the ends had sealed the overlay still said *"the wrap is off: the sheet has two open ends"*, and the side sentence still said *"The last hole closed in 1.00 s."* instead of what Curl did. And the step as it was pressed Curl on whatever the running walk held after the needle step, so whether there was a sheet reaching across the tank to open depended on the machine: on 2026-09-23 it was red on 2 of 5 runs of an unchanged recipe, `taking the wrap away should open two rims: 1.9 -> 0 nm` and `0 -> 0 nm`, the needle's hole having healed as two capped pieces.
- **The mutations and the failures.** Each is `DRIVE_KINDS=bilayer node tools/drive.js`, exit 1.
  - On `034d406`, the figure as it was (`f7cb330`), which reports no `curl`: `FAIL bilayer curl-takes-the-wrap-away-and-the-sheet-rolls-up: in 4 fresh tanks Curl never opened two rims: the last went 1.9 -> 1.9 nm, bilayer`. So the step now needs the figure's own finding, and this arm proves that rather than the words.
  - On `034d406`, the heal-time line keyed on the heal time alone again: `FAIL bilayer curl-takes-the-wrap-away-and-the-sheet-rolls-up: after Curl the reading still speaks of the needle: "What the rule has done Arrangement Bilayer Tails hidden from water 89% … Clock 11.5 s The last hole closed in 1.50 s. Exposed tail edge, last 6 s 4.9 nm now"`.
  - Before the review, on `236778d`, the figure as it was (`f7cb330`): `FAIL bilayer curl-takes-the-wrap-away-and-the-sheet-rolls-up: Curl opened no rims (1.6 -> 1.6 nm) and the overlay did not say so: "the wrap is off: the sheet has two open ends"`.
  - Before the review, on `236778d`, the heal-time line keyed on the heal time alone again (`if (lastEvent === 'needle' && d.healSeconds !== null …` made `if (d.healSeconds !== null …`): `FAIL bilayer curl-takes-the-wrap-away-and-the-sheet-rolls-up: after Curl the reading still speaks of the needle: "What the rule has done Arrangement Bilayer Tails hidden from water 89% … Clock 11.5 s The last hole closed in 1.50 s. Exposed tail edge, last 6 s 4.9 nm now"`.
  - Before the review, on `236778d`, the sealed branch taken out of the overlay (`curlNote()`), not re-run on `034d406`, where that branch is unchanged: `FAIL bilayer curl-takes-the-wrap-away-and-the-sheet-rolls-up: once the rims have closed the overlay should stop calling them open: "the wrap is off: the sheet has two open ends"`.
- **The restoration.** On `034d406`: `src/figures/bilayer.js` sha256 `6dc7e401b38d0d3ef0b7ba723a4d232a4582795861b79e450142ec9b943a78c5` before both arms and after, `git status --short` empty. On `236778d`: the `f7cb330` arm restored by `git checkout src/figures/bilayer.js`, `git status --short` empty; the two mutations ran in a second worktree checked out at `236778d`: `src/figures/bilayer.js` sha256 `4b90ababddc922d3481cf5b6cf5beac365eca3fe0c806743fac25ea2fcccb464` before and after, `git status --short` empty. Green: `drive: 7 steps over 1 figures passed`, exit 0, on `034d406` and on `47f7497`.
- **What it does not prove.** The branch where Curl finds a sheet that does not reach across (`curl: 'none'`): in every green run the first fresh tank opened, so the "no ends opened" assertion has never run against a passing figure. The phone layout's words: its overlay (`the wrap is off: ends sealed`, `no ends opened`) was looked at in frames at 390 px and is not asserted. That what the figure calls a sheet looks like one: at the lab's shape the walk's "sheet" is often two capped aggregates, and the words describe the exposed edge, not the shape.

## unit: at 90 °C the bilayer tank stays dispersed at the flattest shape the figure draws (`test/bilayer-model.test.js`)

- **The tree.** `9483450`, branch `ch04-figs`.
- **Claim**, from the test's own header: the held verdict is `dispersed` at every read, every 0.05 s over 20 s of clock, at 90 °C, for seeds 11, 12 and 13, at `MIN_RATIO` of the tank's width — the flattest, most crowded tank the figure will draw — and the same tank at 37 °C holds a bilayer, so the first three cannot pass on a model that never assembles. **Bound**: those three seeds, 20 s rather than 30 because it runs on every `npm run unit` (about 12 s of it), phospholipid only, the floor alone.
- **Why it was needed.** The drive step above cannot see any tank but the lab's. The tank's height follows its pane, and until this round its floor was 0.52 of its width, which a chapter page at 1024 px reached: a 10.4 nm tank, where one sweep at 90 °C was a sheet or micelles in 89% of reads and the held verdict said so in 22 of 22 runs. Measured in Node with the held verdict over nine seeds and 30 s each, a tank came together at 90 °C in 3 runs at 12.0 nm, 1 at 12.4 nm and none at 12.8 nm, which still came together once at 88 °C; at 13.2 nm no run did at 88 °C or 90 °C. The floor is now 0.66, 13.2 nm, and a flatter pane is given the whole tank, drawn narrower.
- **The mutation and the failure.** `const MIN_RATIO = 0.66;` changed to `0.52` in `src/figures/bilayer.js`. `node --test --experimental-test-isolation=none test/bilayer-model.test.js`, exit 1: all three 90 °C tests failed, *"at 90 °C nothing should assemble, but the tank reported 384 of 400 reads as something else, first: bilayer at t=0.85, bilayer at t=0.90, bilayer at t=0.95, bilayer at t=1.00, bilayer at t=1.05"* for seed 11, 381 of 400 for seed 12 and 384 of 400 for seed 13; the 37 °C test passed, so what failed was the claim and not the model.
- **The restoration.** The constant put back; `git diff` against the working tree then showed only the edits `9483450` committed, and the test passed 4 of 4.
- **What it does not prove.** The drawing, the four other molecules, 85-89 °C, and any window longer than 20 s. The working copy's line endings changed under `sed -i`, so the file's sha256 before and after differ; the content check is the `git diff`.

## legible: chapter 4's eight figures are measured, not deferred (`tools/legible.js`)

- **The tree.** `4b6899b` for the red run, which only took the eight kinds off `DEFERRED`; the green run on `236778d`, with the fixes from branch `ch04-contrast` (`3635940`) merged.
- **Claim**, from the gate's own header, now over chapter 4 too: every glyph a figure puts in the DOM clears WCAG 2.x AA against the pixels under it, in both themes, at 1000x640, in the opening state and after one press of each visible enabled button. **Bound**: that stage size and those states. A glyph's pixels are found inside its bounding box, so a neighbour drawn across it is scored as its surface: the worker who fixed `permeability` read its ion-symbol failures as exactly that — a neighbouring ion's water shell and charge badge over the symbol — from the code and the exact colours, not by a controlled experiment.
- **Why it was needed.** The eight kinds had been deferred since 2026-09-16 because another worker was still writing them, with "13 pairs under AA" carried as their number.
- **The red run, on the real figures.** `LEGIBLE_KINDS=bilayer,membrane3d,permeability,osmometer,transport-lab,pump,gradient-battery,bulk-transport node tools/legible.js`, exit 1, `FAIL: 30 problem(s)`: `permeability` 9 light and 2 dark, `transport-lab` 2 and 2, `pump` 1 light, `gradient-battery` 5 light and 9 dark; `bilayer`, `membrane3d`, `osmometer` and `bulk-transport` clean. Two of the thirty, verbatim: `permeability light: "Cl" is 1.00:1 — #faf7f1 on #faf7f1 — and it is drawn at 9.8 px weight 600, so it needs 4.5:1.` and `gradient-battery dark: "symporter" is 2.15:1 — #908a80 on #575652 — and it is drawn at 9.0 px weight 400, so it needs 4.5:1.`
- **Green.** `legible: 6436 glyph run(s) compared over 16 figure/theme pairs at 1000x640`, every line `0 under bar`, exit 0, on `236778d`. No `ALLOWED` entry was added and no palette value changed.
- **What it does not prove.** Contrast at 390 px, which this gate does not render (the frames there were looked at, not measured); the running states; a pair only a slider reaches.

## shot: the shutter waits for the page to stop moving, and the frames are still not byte-reproducible (`tools/shot.js`, `settleAnimations`)

- **The tree.** `5e9c981` plus this round's uncommitted work in the worktree `textbook-shot-repro`, named by the digest of every tracked and unignored file (sha256 over their sha256s). The settle arms below ran on `8d33fc8e6c35c5f3`, 2026-09-22 23:26–23:43 PDT; its `tools/shot.js` (`eb745266561636e1…`) differs from the one committed only in the settle block's comment, which then held a one-line placeholder, and its `src/shell.js` only by a space missing outside a string (`const SUN ='<svg …`), restored since. The acceptance measurement ran on `fb7cba257d61025d`, 2026-09-23 09:32–09:41 PDT, and held still through all three runs. After it, compared file by file against that tree's manifest, only documents and comments changed — `AGENTS.md`, `docs/design/chapter-recipe.md`, the devlog, this file, `docs/learning/defect-register.md`, and comments in `tools/shot.js`, every one of whose non-comment lines is still the settle arms' version — plus one test, `test/trim.test.js`, which draws no frame. The review's fix round after it changed comments in `tools/shot.js` and `tools/lib/browser.js`, two check files and documents, and nothing a frame is drawn from: `SHOT_PAGES=library SHOT_THEMES=dark` on the fix round's final code (tree `6c30a6a04e753b18`, 2026-09-23 11:29 PDT) wrote all three frames byte-identical to the acceptance run's.
- **Claim**, from the header of `tools/shot.js`: a page fails with *"no CSS transition, CSS animation or Web Animation still running 120 animation frames after the audits — the shutter waits for every one of them to finish, so the frame is the settled page and not a point on a fade"*. **Bound**, from the settle block: *"it waits for what `getAnimations()` reports when it looks — CSS transitions, CSS animations and Web Animations on this document. Something a page starts later, on a timer, is not waited for; a canvas or WebGL figure advancing its own clock is not in that list … neither is anything inside an iframe. It makes the frame still. It does not make the frame reproducible."*
- **Why it was needed.** Three full runs of the frozen 2026-09-17 tree left 33 of its 84 frames differing, all 33 on the 12 pages it shares with this tree. Nine were the dark contents pages, their titles photographed mid-fade by the theme flash ([defect-register.md](defect-register.md), 2026-09-22), and eleven were tongjian chapters whose figures start background and border transitions as they mount.
- **The arms, and what each produced.** Every arm is the gate itself (`node --require ./tools/zj-quiet-browser.cjs tools/shot.js`, one `SHOT_LABEL` per run), frames compared byte for byte. The no-wait arm is a copy of `tools/shot.js` with the one line that runs the wait replaced by the result an already-still page returns, and nothing else changed: each replacement had to happen exactly once or the copy was not written.
  - The nine dark contents-page frames (`SHOT_PAGES=library,biology,tongjian SHOT_THEMES=dark`), eight runs of four arms, interleaved so that every arm saw the same machine over time. Block and wait: **9 of 9 frames identical in all eight runs**. Block, no wait: 9 of 9 identical — on these pages the wait no longer changes a pixel; what it waited for in the first arm, on 39 of its 72 loads, was the reading-progress bar's 80 ms `width` transition from `0` to `0%`, which draws nothing. No block, no wait: **1 of 9 identical, 8 differing, max |delta| up to 90/255 — red, the defect reproduced.** No block, wait: 9 of 9 identical, so the wait holds that class on its own. The three pages were stripped of the block and restored from byte copies in each of the eight iterations, and the tree came back to `8d33fc8e6c35c5f3` every time (`restored: tree=8d33fc8e6c35c5f3 equals pristine`, eight lines).
  - The tongjian chapters (`SHOT_PAGES=tongjian/ch01,tongjian/ch02,tongjian/ch03`, both themes, all three widths), five runs of two arms. With the wait: **18 of 18 identical**. Without it: **8 of 18 identical, 10 differing** (max |delta| 1 to 6) — **the red arm on this tree**, because the block cannot reach these: the transitions are the figures' own.
  - The bound. The library's eyebrow given an infinite animation (`<style>/* MUTATION (landing bound proof) */ @keyframes landingpulse { from { opacity: 1 } to { opacity: .5 } } .library__eyebrow { animation: landingpulse 1s infinite alternate; }</style>` before `</head>` in `index.html`), one load (`SHOT_PAGES=library SHOT_VIEWPORTS=phone SHOT_THEMES=light`). With the wait, exit 1, in 3.1 s: `FAIL library phone light (2472 ms)` / `the page was still moving 120 animation frames after the handshake and the audits, so there is no one frame of it to photograph: animation landingpulse on p.library__eyebrow (2167 ms of infinite). A screenshot of a page in motion is a different picture on every run. What would satisfy this: let it finish inside 120 frames, pin it under ?t=0 the way the figures' clocks are pinned, or, for a finite animation that is meant to be longer, raise SETTLE_MAX_FRAMES in tools/shot.js with the reason.` Without it, exit 0: `ok   library phone light (477 ms, 0 figures ready, 0 sitting(s), still at once (0 ms), …)` — the same page passed as clean.
- **The restoration.** `index.html` sha256 `36325f91408e792390b513544df6d1cb56dad8dce70d0c533263abe47b19e93b` before the bound mutation and after it; the tree back to `8d33fc8e6c35c5f3`.
- **What it costs.** A mean of 15.5 ms a load on the contents pages (max 42 ms, at most 3 frames) and 93 ms on the tongjian chapters (max 191 ms, at most 12 frames); over a whole untrimmed run, 1.9 to 2.9 s for 72 loads. In the three full runs below it waited on 72 of 216 loads: the progress bar on 20, tongjian/ch01 and ch03 on 33, and on 19 a `.fig-btn` changing state as a figure mounts on biology chapters 3, 4 and 5.
- **The measurement that the frames are still not byte-reproducible, class by class.** Three full untrimmed runs of `npm run shot`, one after another, on `fb7cba257d61025d`: each exit 0 with `72 page loads clean`, in 163, 165 and 187 s, no other gate running at the start of any. **61 of 72 frames byte-identical in all three runs; 11 differing in at least one; 8 differing in each of the three pairs.** The population is this tree's 12 pages — the library, both contents pages, biology chapters 1 to 5, tongjian chapters 1 to 3 and Today — at three widths in two themes. On the frozen 2026-09-17 tree the same 12 pages gave **39 of 72 identical, 33 differing, and 27, 24, 27 per pair**; that tree also held biology chapters 6 and 7, whose 12 frames were identical in all three runs, so over its own 84 frames it was 51 (counted from the baseline's per-run hashes, `out/review-runs/r7-baseline-12.log`). No Today frame, contents page, tongjian chapter or phone frame differed. The instrument is a scratch PNG decoder with a band differ, so its five controls (identity, lossless round trip, 12,345 planted +1 deltas, one planted +37, and agreement with sha256) were re-run on these frames first and all returned their stated numbers, and it decoded seven of these frames byte-identically to pngjs and agreed with it on five pairs. Each band of each differing frame was then split into clusters of differing pixels and classified by what the page has under it, read on the gate's own load path:

  | Class | Mechanism | Frames | Size in these runs |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | SVG label advances | a figure label's glyph advances move by 1/64 px from load to load: Chromium keys its font cache on the shaped size to 0.01 px, in float arithmetic, so two neighbouring sizes can share an entry and whichever is shaped first wins (inferred from measurement; a page with no book code on it reproduces it), and the figures' whole-pixel pane boxes put labels at 0.998 to 1.001 of their size. Owner's decision pending between fix G (`text-rendering: geometricPrecision` on figure SVG text) and fix U (unrounded pane boxes) | 3: `biology/ch02` desktop dark and light, `biology/ch05` desktop light | 50 of the 84 clusters, every one on a figure's SVG `<text>` (`carbonkit`, `foldlab`, `phlab`, `polymer`; `entropy`, `free-energy`, `atp`, `coupling`, `enzyme`, `kinetics`, `metabolism`); up to 4,250 px in a frame, channel delta up to 74/255. For 13 of the 18 bands the key model finds the label's partner size in the final DOM; the other five sit on chapter-2 labels with no partner there. On that page the prototype found partners in a figure's provisional first draw (`polymer`), and this measurement did not re-check which labels those were |
  | `backdrop-filter` | the figure toolbars' `.fig-btn` (`blur(8px)`) and `microscopes`' `.mi-state` chip (`blur(6px)`) do not rasterise bit-exactly across loads; in `tools/figure-diff.js`'s header since 2026-09-16 | 6: `biology/ch02` desktop light and tablet in both themes, `biology/ch03` desktop in both themes and tablet light | 18 clusters, 18 to 67 px in a frame, channel delta at most 2 |
  | the first stage's rounded corners | the opening figure's stage clips with `border-radius: 12px` and `overflow: hidden`, and pixels on the curve vary: `soup`, `microscopes`, `bilayer`, `entropy`. What varies inside the corner is not known | 8: `biology/ch02` desktop light and tablet in both themes, `biology/ch03` desktop in both themes, `biology/ch04` tablet dark, `biology/ch05` desktop and tablet dark | 15 clusters, 3 to 13 px a corner, channel delta at most 2 |
  | unexplained | a speck on the rounded right end of `soup`'s temperature control (`.sp-temp`, a pill with a translucent background) — the shape of the two classes above, on an element that neither clips nor blurs | 1: `biology/ch02` desktop light | 1 cluster, 21 px, channel delta 2 |

  Measured before and **not seen** in these runs: a run-level glyph event in which every Latin glyph on a page came back slightly different (3 of 42 trimmed runs on 2026-09-22, cause unexplained), and `soup`'s world size depending on when the fonts arrive (475 against 474 water molecules on the phone layout; [defect-register.md](defect-register.md), no gate yet), which can show only when a chapter 2 load is the first of a run. Looked at, at native resolution: the three dark contents pages at desktop width, whose titles are at their settled ink with nothing mid-fade; and `biology/ch02-desktop-dark.png`, whose largest band (3,685 px, max 74) is `foldlab`'s legend line and shows at 4× as the glyphs drifting by a fraction of a pixel along the line, the same text, nothing a reader can see at native size.
- **What it does not prove.** That a frame is reproducible: it is not, as measured above, so a hash of `out/shots/` is not a regression signal, and `tools/figure-diff.js`'s recorded masks are the repository's answer where figure frames must be compared. Anything a page starts on a timer after the wait has looked, a canvas or WebGL clock (pinned under `?t=0`), or an iframe. On the contents pages its red arm exists only with the theme block taken out: with the block in place it changes no pixel there, and it is kept for what the tongjian chapters and biology chapters 3 to 5 still start as they load.

## theme: the reader's theme is on `<html>` before `<body>` exists, on every page that loads the shell (`tools/theme.js`)

- **The tree.** As in the entry below: `5e9c981` plus this round's uncommitted work. The green run and the four page arms were re-run on 2026-09-23, 11:28–11:29 PDT, on the `tools/theme.js` that the review's finding R4 left, on tree `6c30a6a04e753b18` (the digest of every tracked and unignored file), the same before and after each arm; the R4 arm ran at 11:22 on the same `tools/theme.js`, tree `5062cd8799913d38`. The runs of 10:23–10:25 (tree `babafe2758282ca0`), on the version before R4, gave the same verdicts; the first worker's runs of 2026-09-22 predate both and are not the proof.
- **Claim**, from the header of `tools/theme.js`: on every published page that loads the shell, in seven situations, *"`data-theme` on <html> is the theme the page is to be drawn in from the moment <body> is inserted to the moment the handshake is ready — at every instant of that interval, not only at its two ends — and it is the value the shell leaves on <html> there"*, and the page reaches the handshake with no console error, page error or failed request, showing its content. Its instruments are checked too: the emulated colour scheme must read back as asked, the observer must have seen `<body>` inserted, and in the two throwing situations the page's own code must have hit the throw. **Bound**, from the same header: *"Chromium only, one viewport (1024x768), and no `?eager=1` … It proves the attribute … It cannot see a flash that happens in the right theme — a stylesheet or a font arriving late — and says nothing about WebKit or Gecko."*
- **Why it was needed.** The theme flash, [defect-register.md](defect-register.md), 2026-09-22: one to three frames of the whole page in the other theme, then the titles fading across, on up to 6 loads of 6.
- **The green run.** `npm run theme`, untrimmed, exit 0 in 31 s: `theme: 91 load(s) clean, 7 situation(s) on each of 13 page(s) — every page that loads the shell: biology/ch01, biology/ch02, biology/ch03, biology/ch04, biology/ch05, biology, library, lab, today, tongjian/ch01, tongjian/ch02, tongjian/ch03, tongjian`. Every clean line says how many changes the interval held, so a page whose attribute was never written after `<body>` does not read like one that was written and checked: `ok   biology/ch01 stored-dark-on-light (467 ms, system light, at <body> "dark", 1 change(s) between <body> and the handshake, every one from "dark", after the handshake "dark", …)`.
- **The mutations, and the exact failures.** Each page mutated alone, then `THEME_PAGES=<that page> npm run theme`, seven loads, the run's last line naming what it judged and what it did not:
  - **delete**, `biology/index.html`, the block's two lines removed — the page exactly as it was on `5e9c981`. Exit 1, `FAIL: 4 of 7 load(s) had problems — a trimmed run: judged biology; …`, the four being the situations with a choice to apply. The first: `FAIL biology stored-dark-on-light (402 ms)` / `data-theme on <html> was absent when <body> was inserted, where this page should be drawn "dark" from its first frame. Nothing had applied the reader's theme by then, so the page is first styled in the system's "light" and the shell switches it to "dark" later: that is the flash, and then every title with a colour transition fading across. The page's <head> is missing the block from src/theme-early.js, carries a copy that has drifted from it, or no longer runs it before <body> — test/theme-early.test.js says which, and prints the block.` The same sentence, theme and system swapped as the situation has them, for `stored-light-on-dark`, `url-dark-on-light` and `url-light-on-dark`; `nothing-chosen`, `getitem-throws` and `storage-blocked` stay `ok`, correctly, with nothing to apply.
  - **drift**, `today/index.html`, the key in the page's copy changed from `'tb-theme'` to `'tb-thme'`. Exit 1, `FAIL: 2 of 7 load(s) had problems`: `stored-dark-on-light` and `stored-light-on-dark`, with the sentence above. The two `?theme=` situations stay `ok`, because the drifted copy still reads the URL: only a stored choice exposes this drift, which is why both kinds of situation are in the seven.
  - **unguarded**, `lab/index.html`, the copy's `try { … } catch (e) {}` removed. Exit 1, `FAIL: 2 of 7 load(s) had problems`: `FAIL lab getitem-throws (90 ms)` / `pageerror: The operation is insecure.` and `FAIL lab storage-blocked (85 ms)` / `pageerror: Access is denied for this document.`
  - **late**, `tongjian/index.html`, the block moved from the top of `<head>` to just before `</head>`, after every stylesheet. **Exit 0, `theme: 7 load(s) clean`** — `ok   tongjian stored-dark-on-light (545 ms, system light, at <body> "dark", 1 change(s) between <body> and the handshake, every one from "dark", after the handshake "dark", …)`. Recorded as the bound it is: a classic script at the end of `<head>` still runs before `<body>` is inserted, so the attribute is right and this gate cannot tell where the block sits. The text check fails it (the entry below).
  - **removed and put back** (review finding R4), `src/shell.js`: `delete document.documentElement.dataset.theme; await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 50));` inserted just before the shell reads `?theme=`, so after `<body>` exists the attribute the block set is taken away and the shell puts it back 50 ms later. `THEME_PAGES=library`. **The gate as reviewed passed it**, exit 0, `theme: 7 load(s) clean`, `ok   library stored-dark-on-light (375 ms, system light, at <body> "dark", after the handshake "dark", …)`: it read the attribute at two instants, and both were right. The gate as it now stands fails it, exit 1, `FAIL: 4 of 7 load(s) had problems`, on every situation with a theme to apply: `FAIL library stored-dark-on-light (386 ms)` / `after <body> was inserted, data-theme changed 2 time(s) before the handshake, and between change 1 and change 2 it was absent, where this page is drawn "dark" from <body> to the handshake. For that stretch the page was styled in another theme: the flash again, in the middle of the load instead of before it. Something after the block in <head> removed or replaced the attribute and then put it back; src/shell.js's applyTheme is where the page writes it, and during a load it may only write the value the block already set.` The three situations with nothing to apply stay `ok`, each with `0 change(s) between <body> and the handshake`. `src/shell.js` sha256 `1edaf22f24c10e86…` before and after, the tree `5062cd8799913d38` before and after.
  - **The trimming variable.** `THEME_PAGES=ch01 node tools/theme.js` stops in 575 ms, before the server or the browser starts, exit 1: `Error: THEME_PAGES=ch01 names no such page: "ch01". A name that matches nothing would run nothing, and a gate that runs nothing must not pass. The pages are: biology/ch01, biology/ch02, biology/ch03, biology/ch04, biology/ch05, biology, library, lab, today, tongjian/ch01, tongjian/ch02, tongjian/ch03, tongjian. Page ids carry their book: biology/ch01, not ch01.` And `test/trim.test.js`'s list of gates that must read their trimming variables through `trim()` now includes `theme`: with `tools/theme.js` mutated to split `THEME_PAGES` itself, it fails with `tools/theme.js splits a variable itself: process.env.THEME_PAGES.split(; read it through trim() in tools/lib/trim.js so a value naming nothing fails`.
- **The restoration.** Each page restored from a byte copy, sha256 the same before and after: `biology/index.html` `30278962da69edbc…`, `today/index.html` `d47638d48cd89f40…`, `tongjian/index.html` `43c0de8618e28ab6…`, `lab/index.html` `a41d8f8475d3fd71…`; the tree was `6c30a6a04e753b18` before and after each of the four page arms. `tools/theme.js` was `b096421611d4752a…` before the trim mutation (10:18 PDT, before the R4 change to this file) and after it.
- **What it does not prove.** Where in `<head>` the block sits: the fourth arm is green, so the placement is held by `test/theme-early.test.js` alone. The pixels: that `tokens.css` answers the attribute with the right colours is `test/palette.test.js`'s and `test/theme.test.js`'s. A flash in the right theme. WebKit and Gecko, which run a classic script at the same point of the parse but are not loaded here. It holds one attribute over one interval, from `<body>` being inserted to the handshake: anything else in that interval, and anything after the handshake, is outside it.

## unit: every page that loads the shell carries the theme block, verbatim, before its first stylesheet (`test/theme-early.test.js`)

- **The tree.** `5e9c981` plus this round's uncommitted work: the block in the 13 pages, `src/theme-early.js`, the import in `src/shell.js`, `tools/lib/shell-pages.js`, this test and `tools/theme.js`. Every page arm was re-run on 2026-09-23, 11:28–11:29 PDT, on the version of this file that the review's finding R3 left, on tree `6c30a6a04e753b18`, the same before and after each arm; the R3 arm ran at 11:20. The runs of 10:23–10:25 (tree `babafe2758282ca0`), before R3, gave the same failures, and the first worker's runs of 2026-09-22 predate both.
- **Claim**, from the test's header: discovery finds every published page whose modules reach `src/shell.js`, derived a second way from the gates' own `PAGES` and a plain search for the shell's two entry modules, and *"a page that reaches the shell only through its own script, loaded by a relative `src`, is found too; neither derivation can see that case, so a fixture holds it"*; each such page carries `THEME_BLOCK` *"exactly once, character for character after carriage returns are dropped, outside any comment, inside <head>, and before its first stylesheet"*, and *"the failure names each page, says which of those it broke, and prints the block to paste and where it goes"*; the shell takes its storage key from `src/theme-early.js`; and the block's own script, run in a sandbox, puts `?theme=` before the stored choice, stores nothing, and neither throws nor sets anything when storage throws. **Bound**, from the same header: *"text and a sandbox. It proves each page carries the block and where; that the block runs before <body> exists on the real page, and agrees with what the shell leaves there, is `npm run theme`'s."*
- **Why it was needed.** A page cannot `import` the block, because it has to run before any module could, so every page carries a copy, and a copy is what goes missing on a new page or drifts on an old one. Chapters 6, 7 and 8 are being written on branches that will rebase onto this and meet the failure cold, so it names the page, prints the block and says where it goes.
- **The mutations, and the exact failures.** Each `node --test test/theme-early.test.js`, exit 1, one of five tests failing. After the page's own line, every failure goes on with the same explanation and the block to paste, quoted once here: `What the block is for: src/shell.js applies the reader's stored theme (or ?theme=) only after the page has been styled once, so a page without the block shows a reader whose choice differs from their system the whole page in the other theme for a few frames, and then its titles fade across. The block applies the same choice before anything is styled.` / `Paste these lines exactly as they are into the page's <head>, BEFORE its first stylesheet (<link rel="stylesheet"> or <style>) — every page in the tree carries them on the line after <meta name="viewport" …>:` / the two lines of the block / `Never edit a page's copy. To change the block, change THEME_BLOCK in src/theme-early.js and paste the new text into every page this test names.`
  - **delete**, `biology/index.html`, the block's two lines removed. The mutated page hashes to `776ff309699c393d…`, the bytes that page had on `5e9c981` before the block went in, so this arm is the real defect on the real file: `1 of 13 page(s) that load the shell do not carry the theme block as they must:` / `biology/index.html does not carry the block at all.`
  - **drift**, `today/index.html`, the key in the page's copy changed from `'tb-theme'` to `'tb-thme'`: `today/index.html carries a copy of the block that differs from THEME_BLOCK in src/theme-early.js. Its line 7 parts from the block at column 155:` / `page:  …orage.getItem('tb-thme'); if (t) document.documentElement.da…` / `block: …orage.getItem('tb-theme'); if (t) document.documentElement.d…` / `Replace the page's copy with the block below, whole.`
  - **late**, `tongjian/index.html`, the block moved to just before `</head>`: `tongjian/index.html carries the block on line 110, after its first stylesheet on line 14; a script after a stylesheet that is still loading waits for it, so the block has to come first.` The browser gate stays green on this one (the entry above), so this is the only check that holds the placement.
  - **unguarded**, `lab/index.html`, the copy's `try { … } catch (e) {}` removed: `lab/index.html carries a copy of the block that differs from THEME_BLOCK in src/theme-early.js. Its line 7 parts from the block at column 24:` / `page:  …ript>(function () { var t = new URLSearchParams(location.sea…` / `block: …ript>(function () { try { var t = new URLSearchParams(locati…` / `Replace the page's copy with the block below, whole.`
  - **a relative `src`** (review finding R3). Not a mutation of the tree: the fixture `test/fixtures/shell-pages/` is the defect — `lab/index.html` loads its own script by `<script type="module" src="lab.js">`, and `lab/lab.js` imports the shell through `../src/components/index.js`, beside `plain/`, whose script reaches nothing — and the red run is the walker as reviewed against it, 4 pass and 1 fail: `tools/lib/shell-pages.js did not follow the fixture page lab/index.html to the shell: it returned null, where the page loads lab.js by <script type="module" src="lab.js"> and lab.js imports ../src/components/index.js, which imports ../shell.js. A <script src> is a URL resolved against the page, so "lab.js" is lab/lab.js; only an import specifier goes through the import map when it is bare.` With `src` resolved as a URL relative to the page, and the bare-specifier rule kept for imports: `fixture: lab/index.html -> lab/lab.js -> src/components/index.js -> src/shell.js`, `plain/` not found, and the real tree's list of 13 pages the same before and after the fix.
  - Green on the restored tree, 5 of 5: `13 page(s) load the shell: biology/ch01-what-is-life/index.html, …, tongjian/index.html`, `fixture: lab/index.html -> lab/lab.js -> src/components/index.js -> src/shell.js`, `13 page(s) checked, each carrying the block once, in <head>, before its first stylesheet`, `12 case(s) run`.
- **The restoration.** Each page restored from a byte copy, sha256 the same before and after: `biology/index.html` `30278962da69edbc…`, `today/index.html` `d47638d48cd89f40…`, `tongjian/index.html` `43c0de8618e28ab6…`, `lab/index.html` `a41d8f8475d3fd71…`; the tree `6c30a6a04e753b18` before and after every arm.
- **What it does not prove.** That the block runs before `<body>` on the real page, or agrees with the shell there: `npm run theme`'s. The sandbox case compares the block with the rule as written in its header, not with the shell's code; the browser gate is what compares the two. A page that reaches the shell only through an import whose specifier is computed at runtime is not found by the walk. The match is exact, so a copy reformatted but equivalent fails, which is intended.

## flow / mark-audit: every marked run on a second-book chapter page is visibly marked, on the rendered page (`tools/flow.js`, `mark-audit`)

Taken in the worktree `textbook-wt-provenance` (branch `provenance-quality`) off `main` at `3d3be2c`, with `tongjian/zj.css` as the round's own fix left it. Restorations are byte for byte; the page mutated here is `tongjian/ch03-cai-de-lun/index.html`, sha256 `B4E578DDA0678992818A62053CA466A48534CEADF50DCAEF7ABBEFF3D56AE423` before and after (mutated `1EB2604A76BDF91A7C0C9042634352CF28CFED6C0B6E4750E34EC759CF1A8E83`), and `src/components/sort.js` `1832BA4580160986160C582EAF6B2C5A0F4C8B1BB8C8BA7E6B6F0C9E1A2F2D4A` before and after (mutated `B58BC160…`).

- **Why it was needed.** The provenance change's claim is that a run of 資治通鑑's words inside the book's prose is set so a reader can see whose words they are — and nothing measured that on the rendered page. `test/provenance.test.js` reads **markup** and says so in its own header, and an independent typographic review then found the page disagreeing with the markup in three places: five runs in chapter 3's `<tb-check>` options with **no mark at all** (`src/components/check.js` rebuilds each option as a `<button>`, so the run sat in a container the rule's whitelist did not match), a textual note's run repainted out of the note's own 青, and no ink step in any `--ink-soft` container. The second and third are 17 of the book's 42 marked runs.
- Claim (the step's own header): walking the rendered page, for every element carrying `lang="zh-Hant"` inside that page's reading column, either one of the stylesheet's `[lang="zh-Hant"]` rules reaches it and its computed size, colour or weight differs from its container's, or it sits in a region the rules deliberately exclude. The rules are read **out of `tongjian/zj.css`** — the scope from the front of the first one and the excluded regions from its `:not(…)` clauses — so the gate and the stylesheet cannot drift apart.
- Bound, from the header and the worker's own list: one viewport, one theme and the opening state (1440 px light, because `?eager=1&t=0` pins figures); it cannot see a mark that **repaints a deliberately coloured container** (pre-fix, the 異文 run went `--ink-soft` against the note's 青 and still "differed" — that is a statement about which colour a container should keep, and there was no rule in the file to read it from); nor a difference too small to see; nor a run whose container is not what the reader compares it against; nor text drawn into a canvas. It visits chapter pages only, so **the key page's own mark is not held by anything**.
- **The mutation, and it is the review's own defect.** Inside the first `<li data-correct>` of chapter 3's q-weizhi, the run the review measured was given `style="font-size: inherit; color: inherit"` — the markup still says marked, the page shows no mark, which is exactly the rendered symptom. `npm run flow`, exit 1:
  ```text
  FAIL mark-audit [tongjian/ch03]: /tongjian/ch03-cai-de-lun/: 1 of 40 marked run(s) is not visibly marked; first: the run "臣光曰" in <span < span < li.data-correct ... > — it matches rule 0 and yet is identical to its container in size, colour and weight; run 17px / rgb(29, 26, 23) / weight 400, container 17px / rgb(29, 26, 23) / weight 400
  ```
  The numbers are the review's own measurement of the defect. Restored byte for byte, then green.
- **Two more arms.** `src/components/sort.js` `card.draggable = true` → `false` fails all four drag steps (`FAIL sort-by-drag [biology/ch01-what-is-life]: the dragged card did not land in the Not alive bin`, and the same for `tongjian/ch01`, `ch02`, `ch03`) — the biology book is in this file too. And a `<tb-sort>`'s `<ul class="items">` renamed on `tongjian/ch01` fails `subject-present` **naming the selector and the page**, with only that page's steps skipped. A `pointer-events: none` mutation on the bins did **not** redden the drag: Chromium's HTML5 drop ignores it, which is a useful negative rather than a hole.
- **The green run**: `flow: 37 steps passed; pages driven: biology/ch01-what-is-life, tongjian/ch01, tongjian/ch02, tongjian/ch03`, with each chapter's census printed — `ch01 4 run(s); 2/0/0 by rule; 2 in an excluded region; 0 not visibly marked`, `ch02 73; 15/7/1; 51; 0`, `ch03 40; 13/9/0; 18; 0`.
- **What the worker found while proving it, and fixed**: its first subject table counted across the whole page, so chapter 3's other four checks masked a broken first one and the step burned its full 180 s timeout on a locator that could never match; the table is now per-root. And the scope guard earned its place on the first run by **refusing to audit** when it read a mid-write `zj.css` whose first `[lang="zh-Hant"]` rule was the old whitelist shape, rather than silently matching the wrong runs — reading a live file another session is writing is a real fragility in this design and is named rather than assumed away.

## provenance: a 通鑑 quotation from outside the page's own passage names the work it came from (`test/provenance.test.js`, checks 6 to 8)

Taken in the worktree `textbook-wt-gate` off `main` at `741bfa7`, landed as `3ad9ef9`. Ch02's page hash `4b90ce60a1442759d1c388cc434fce81c2c00b46ed4405374fb291ef2eb02007` before the red arms and after each restore; test file `bf091d70b111e64dd1e5c71356360209b7db86e4119e57f1e685e72662c8e8dc`. Evidence under the ignored `out/citation-gate/` (`probe3`–`probe6.mjs` measured the predicate; `red-proof5.mjs` + `red-proof5.log` are the arms).

- **Why it was needed, and it is the last objective item.** The owner asked why a 通鑑 sentence quoted in 背景 was not also in the 原文 section, and was told 原文 is a *selection*; he then asked for it to be made perfectly clear what came from the book and what did not, everywhere. Everything else in that answer was built and gated. What was not: **a page could make a claim about its own provenance that no check could see** — the sentence that started it said 「所以这里只转述，不引原文」 four lines above a figure that quoted the passage. The design answer was to give a context quotation a visible citation and to state the selection rule once in the key; that made the citation *available* and nothing made it *mandatory*.
- Claim (the file's own header): a marked run in a page's prose whose text is **not** part of any of that page's own `<ol class="zj-src" data-corpus="…">` passages must sit in a citing block **that names the work** (`《资治通鉴》`/`《通鑑》`/bracketless 通鑑 allowed). Beside it, two claims that were unchecked: **a figure row printing a `quote` carries a non-empty `quoteAt`/`source`** (15 objects read, 8 printing a quotation, 8 of 8 citing it) and **no sentence claims the book leaves a passage out while printing that passage** (201 sentences, 2 claiming an exclusion, 0 printing 通鑑 in the same breath).
- **The predicate was measured, and the sharper forms were rejected with numbers.** Requiring a `.zj-at`, a 卷 or a 年 fires on **8** runs and is wrong on **4** — a chapter that names a passage's place in the book's own order (「原书的次序是「初，智宣子將以瑤為後」一路写到「唯輔果在」」) is discussing the order, not quoting it, and naming a passage is not quoting it. "Not in the selection" alone is worse: **12** fires, 9 of them with no defect. The shipped form needs the citing block to name the work: of 58 marked runs in the three chapters' prose, 45 are part of the page's own selection, **13 are quoted from elsewhere and all 13 name the work; 0 that do not**. All three populations print on every run, so a `0` cannot read as "everything".
- **Four red arms, each run on the final revision, each asserting its own mutation is the defect and not a no-op** — because two earlier versions of the harness reported *green* arms as proofs, their anchors having matched text already inside the sentence carrying the claim:
  1. a context quotation added with no source (ch02 line 313):
     ```text
     FAIL: tongjian\ch02-zhi-bo-zhi-wang\index.html:313: 「起著雍攝提格，盡玄黓困敦，凡三十五年」 is quoted from outside this page's own 原文 selection and the paragraph it stands in never names 通鑑 — a reader is given 通鑑's words with nothing saying whose they are
     ```
  2. the same shape on a second page (ch01 line 132), so it is not the same mutation twice;
  3. an emptied `quoteAt`:
     ```text
     src\figures\zj-timeline.js: `quote` prints 「魏、韓、趙共廢晉靖公為家人而分其地。」 and the row carries no non-empty quoteAt or source, so the figure prints 通鑑 with no citation on it
     ```
  4. the false sentence restored, printing what it excludes:
     ```text
     tongjian\ch02-zhi-bo-zhi-wang\index.html: the sentence says this book leaves a passage out and prints 通鑑 in the same breath — 「唯輔果在」
     ```
  Every arm restored byte for byte, hashes identical before and after.
- **One real fix, and it was invisible to half the check.** Chapter 2's opener dek quoted 智伯之亡也，才勝德也 with no source in its paragraph; it now names 卷一 and the 「臣光曰」 the words come from. Found only by the "does the block name the work" half — every chapter's own selection carries 「臣光曰」, so the run reads as *in selection* — and that bound is written into the header.
- **What it does not prove**: it reads the page's markup, so a citation built at runtime is invisible; it cannot tell a page naming a work for another reason from one citing a quotation; the exclusion-sentence check matches a clause list, so a claim phrased outside it is not caught; and four rewording candidates the predicate deliberately does not flag are listed in the round's report rather than changed.

## flow / phone-figure-citation: a figure that prints 通鑑's words draws the citation that says whose they are (`tools/flow.js`)

Taken across three worktrees — `textbook-wt-figure`, `textbook-wt-provenance` and the earlier `textbook-wt-provenance` revision — on `main` at `741bfa7`, landed as `3ad9ef9` and `f611760`. Restoration hashes, each identical before and after: zj-timeline `42ac4da7…2c74` before, `0e45a2058d8cd13d96edd2928ce22fc2ee2efb1ae21a35f0238ca2a27f547ab2` after the overlap round; zj-split `a0a942e62d190e2611d558f53e51a131e0b12dff4fe9abfb405bcb82a346d5f8`; zj-words `9c60468bce29daddcc16757a89b2181535b3d0687ebe239865a1884e4f8b9521`.

- **Why it was needed.** Figure 2.1 printed 通鑑's sentence and dropped its citation by rule in the narrow compositions, so **at 390 px a reader met the book's words with nothing saying whose they were** — on the figure whose subject is where these words sit. Measured then: `12.0 px not drawn`. The same defect was in the book's other two figures (`.zjs-source`, `.zjw-caption`), and none of the three published a field a checker could ask about, so `phone-figure-citation` read two of them as `0 showing a quotation`.
- Claim: on the phone load, any figure whose own `describe()` reports `quoted` must **draw** the citation its kind declares — not `hidden`, not `display:none`/`visibility:hidden`, a non-zero box, type at or above a 10 px floor — and a quoting figure whose kind is not declared **fails** rather than being skipped, with a run-level census failing a declared kind no page exercised.
- **Measured after**: at 390×585 (the stage a reader's phone gives) zj-split's citation goes from `display:none`, `0x0` to drawn with its note yielded and **18.7 px clear**; zj-words' from `display:none` to both 卷次 captions drawn with the card intact and **12.8 px clear**; zj-timeline's drawn at the same clearance it had before, note kept. The census went from `exercised zj-timeline x1 of 1 declared kind(s)` to `exercised zj-split x1, zj-timeline x1, zj-words x1 of 3 declared kind(s)`, and every phone page reads `0 without a drawn citation`.
- **Three red arms, one per figure** (the defect reintroduced as `display:none` at the narrow tier), each failing with the same shape:
  ```text
  FAIL phone-figure-citation [tongjian/ch02]: /tongjian/ch02-zhi-bo-zhi-wang/ at 390 px: 1 figure(s) showing 通鑑's words do not draw the citation that says whose words they are; first: <fig-timeline> (zj-timeline) — its ".zjt-quote-at" citation is in the markup but not drawn: display none, visibility visible, box 0.0x0.0 at 12px
  ```
  and the same naming `<fig-split>` (`.zjs-source`) on ch01 and `<fig-words>` (`.zjw-caption`) on ch03. **Two of the six attempts were inert and ran green** — the new `[data-cite="1"]` rule outranks a plainly injected hide — which is recorded in the proof instruments; the arms now assert the mutation is the defect before running.
- **The bound, and it is the part to read**: the check keys on `describe().quoted`, so a figure printing 通鑑 without publishing that field is never asked; it presses no control, so a row the reader must select is unmeasured; it proves a box and a type floor, not legibility against what is under it; it reads one viewport; and **it asks only that the citation be drawn — nothing measures collisions inside a figure**, which is why the panel drawing 42.2 px over its own toolbar at a 320 px stage was found by hand and not by a gate. `narrow`'s collision check reads only the nine bench kinds and `shot` reads console errors and invalid geometry. At that stage two figures still draw over their controls (zj-split −38.7 px, zj-words −35.2 px) and no block they can yield clears it; both files now state it with the numbers.
- **What it does not prove**: that the citation is legible against what is behind it; that the figure is otherwise well composed; or that a figure added tomorrow declares its kind — that fails loudly, which is the point, but only for a figure the table names.

## provenance: a run of 通鑑's own words long enough to be a quotation carries the mark, bracketed or not (`test/provenance.test.js`)

Taken in the worktree `textbook-wt-provenance` (branch `provenance-quality`) off `main` at `9000533`, on the round's own page edits. Baseline ch02 sha256 `0e68a201f76b0176a399a2f5698a7e6a8bd56e5d5ccb558b72e3f7200c0deee1` before each arm and after each restore. Evidence under the ignored `out/provenance-bare/`: `PROOF.md`, `probe.mjs`, `probe2.mjs`, `report1-fixed-pages.txt`, `report2-original-pages.txt`, `report2-fixed-pages.txt`, `paint.mjs`, `terms.mjs`.

- **Why it was needed, and it is the hole the owner's question came in through.** Both existing checks were bounded by the book's own 「」: the page check looked for bracketed runs, and the script check cannot see a run that is Simplified or identical in both scripts, because `test/lexicon.test.js` blanks every `<tb-term>`/`<tb-char>` and every marked run is compared as it stands. So the book could print 通鑑's words bare — as a clause in its own sentence, a sort card's label, a check option — and no gate could tell. The independent typographic reviewer had named it: *"that is the gateway the owner's question came in through — what came from the book and what didn't — and it is still open."* Five such runs were live, and chapter 3's sort card was printing seven of the received text's clauses as its own words.
- Claim (the test's own header): a **maximal run** of a chapter page's prose that is a **verbatim substring of `tongjian/corpus.js`** (or of the two lines outside it), holding **≥ 6 Han characters**, must sit inside a `lang="zh-Hant"` element or inside a 「…」 pair. Runs are found by extending each position as far as the corpus allows — *"is a prefix of this a quotation"* only falls as the prefix grows, so it is a binary search — tags are stripped first so a run the markup splits across a `<span>` is one run, and a run another run contains is dropped.
- **The floor is measured, not chosen.** A probe read **639 maximal corpus runs** in the three chapters' prose: 55 already marked, 73 inside 「」, 566 neither. Grouped by Han count: 9 Han → 1; 7 → 1; 6 → 3; 5 → 2; 4 → 14 (智氏之族, 才德兼亡, 棠溪之金 …); 3 → 35 (卿大夫, 智宣子, 魏桓子 …); 2 → 264 (智伯, 大夫, 晋阳 …). So the gap is between the **term and name population at ≤4** and the **clause population at ≥6**, and 6 is its lower edge; 5 is named in the header as the band the floor is set above rather than silently missed.
- **Bound, and it was measured rather than asserted.** Comparing in **script-normalised space** — folding both page and corpus through the checked-in `test/fixtures/traditional-only.txt` before asking whether one contains the other — **cannot be shipped**, and the numbers are why: on the pre-fix tree, floor 6 fires **12** times, of which **6 are the book's own Simplified voice** (`尽灭智氏之族`, `魏、韩、赵共废晋`, the 世系 sentence, `相亲之兵待轻敌之人，智氏`, `才胜德谓之小人。`, `才有余而德不足，`); floor 10 still fires 3, and **the two longest are the book's own prose**. No floor separates the two classes, and the reason is not tuning: the book's line between its own words and 通鑑's *is* the script, because a quotation is defined as verbatim character for character. So the predicate is script-sensitive by design, and **a 通鑑 run the book prints in Simplified is named in the header as the uncovered bound** rather than caught by a predicate that would fire on the book's own narration. Catching the verbatim case and naming the Simplified case beats firing on the subject matter.
- **Arm A — a Traditional bare run, the case the script check also sees.** `「<span lang="zh-Hant">智伯之亡也，才勝德也</span>」` reduced to the bare Traditional text. Mutant `adcb54779127610473de10ed322be51f9e6fa0c6178870388e7afe0422755abf`. **The new check fails, and so does `test/lexicon.test.js`** — `1 Traditional-only outside the 原文` — which is the useful half: a Traditional bare quotation was already caught by the script rule, so this check's job is the other case. Restored to `0e68a201…`.
- **Arm B — the hole itself: identical in both scripts.** `「<span lang="zh-Hant">智伯之亡也，才勝德也</span>」` → `智伯之亡也，才胜德也`. Mutant `d9e949c9456f3c54ec18b9b9edc1fe625ed153ef4db3986d2f41b654b3e9c8e1`. `test/lexicon.test.js`: **13 tests, 13 pass, 0 fail, 0 Traditional-only outside the 原文**; the previous provenance check: **passes, 0 unmarked**. Only the new check fails, verbatim:
  ```text
  ✖ a run of 通鑑's own words long enough to be a quotation carries the mark, bracketed or not
    AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: 1 run(s) of 通鑑's own words are printed in the book's prose with neither the 「」 that would mark them as a citation nor the lang="zh-Hant" that says whose they are. Either bracket and mark the run in the Traditional script, exactly as tongjian/corpus.js prints it, or say it in the book's own words. See docs/design/tongjian.md, "Which words are the book's".
          tongjian\ch02-zhi-bo-zhi-wang\index.html:58: 「：智伯之亡也，才」 is 通鑑's own text, verbatim in tongjian/corpus.js, and is printed bare — no 「」 and no lang="zh-Hant" — so a reader reads it as the book's own sentence
  ```
  Restored byte for byte to `0e68a201…`. **Two arms and the second one is the point**: without this check the run passes every gate in the repository.
- **The green run and its denominators**: `bare runs: 3 chapter page(s), 376 maximal 通鑑 run(s) read in the prose — 59 marked, 10 inside 「」 and unmarked (check 1's population), 307 bare and under 6 Han characters (the floor's population); 0 bare and at or above it`. The check asserts `read > 0` and `markedRuns > 0`, so a scan that finds nothing fails rather than passing.
- **The five fixes, and one judgement above the finding.** ch02:58's opener dek (`：智伯之亡也，才胜德也。`), ch02:382's `q-chici` explanation (`人马相食，城降有日`), ch03:205's `q-yuren` correct option (`智足以遂其奸，勇足以決其暴` — the corpus prints 奸 and 決, so the run stops at the 、 before 決 and the one Traditional-only character there is 決), ch03:208's `q-yuren` explanation (`愚者虽欲为不善，智不能周，力不能胜`) and ch03:259's sort item (`智不能周，力不能胜`). **And chapter 3's sort card**: its intro asks the reader to sort 每一句 and every `data-why` opens 原文：, so **seven of its nine items are the received text's clauses printed Simplified** and now carry the mark, Traditional and verbatim — 才德全盡, 德勝才, 才德兼亡, 才勝德, 挾才以為善, 挾才以為惡, 智不能周，力不能勝. Two were deliberately **not** marked and the report names them: 智伯 (a name the book uses as its own word) and 德为才之帅 (the book's compression of 「德者，才之帥也」, not verbatim, so marking it would fail `test/lexicon.test.js`). Marking the one fired item and not its six siblings would have told the reader that one clause is 通鑑's and the others, equally his, are the book's.
- **What it does not prove**, beyond the floor and the Simplified bound above: it reads the page's prose, so a quotation inside a figure's data or a card's fields is held by the other checks and not by this one; it cannot tell a run the book is *teaching* from one it is *quoting* except by the script, which is why the Simplified case is a bound and not a check; and it says nothing about whether a fixed run is marked *well* — `tools/flow.js`'s rendered `mark-audit` is what asks whether the mark is visible.

## standing-docs: the two standing records' indexes agree with their entries, both directions (`test/standing-docs.test.js`)

Taken in the worktree `textbook-wt-provenance` (branch `provenance-quality`) off `main` at `3d3be2c`, the only revision this gate has been run on. Baselines for the mutations: `docs/learning/gate-proofs.md` sha256 `73d755dfcbc09b603585f01c252c7c10c477a941ce545e9f9008b6915ff56891`, `docs/learning/defect-register.md` sha256 `ce59a9ff51302b2605b2e053eb3210f9ee0a6ece8d44c0163277277cfa663a6b`. Captures under the ignored `out/standing-docs-arms/` and `out/unit-final.txt`.

- **Why it was needed, and it is a defect of this repository's own round.** Commit `3d3be2c` exists because a new `gate-proofs.md` entry landed with no line in the index — the entry was complete and correct and nobody following the index could reach it. The line had been dropped by the shell command that staged the file: it selected the bullet with PowerShell's `-like` and a pattern beginning `- [a quotation…]`, and in `-like` `[...]` is a character class, so the pattern matched nothing and the line was never staged. Both files open with an index and both tell a reader to read one entry through it, so an entry with no line is an entry nobody finds; nothing in the repository could see either half. **The first run of this gate then found a second one, three commits old**: `gate-proofs.md`'s `legible` entry had no index line, born in `0806283`, the commit that built the grouped index (44 entries, 43 links). It is added in the same commit as this test.
- Claim (the test's own header): every `## ` heading that is an entry is the target of a link somewhere in the index; every index link resolves to a heading; and the populations are not empty — each file must yield headings, entries and links before anything is compared, with every count printed. Anchors are GitHub's slugs, Han characters kept and duplicates suffixed, pinned by literal expected strings rather than derived from the slugger.
- Bound, quoted from the header: it proves the index and the entries **agree**. It does not check that a line's *description* of an entry is accurate, nor whether an entry's own claims are true, nor that a gate an entry describes still exists or still passes. In `gate-proofs.md`'s list index a link resolving to a structural heading is not a failure; only `defect-register.md`'s table is held to "one entry per line". It reads text, so a slug rule it does not model could still differ from GitHub's.
- **Arm A — the defect that happened.** The provenance entry's index bullet deleted. Failure, exit 1:
  ```text
  standing-docs: docs/learning/gate-proofs.md — read 836 lines, 49 headings, 44 entries, 43 index links, 1 entry unreachable, 0 links broken
  AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: standing-docs: docs/learning/gate-proofs.md has 1 entry heading with no line in its index, so a reader who follows the index never reaches it:
    line 86: ## provenance: a quotation of 通鑑 the page does not mark is invisible, and this is what makes it visible (`test/provenance.test.js`)
      add an index line linking to #provenance-a-quotation-of-通鑑-the-page-does-not-mark-is-invisible-and-this-is-what-makes-it-visible-testprovenancetestjs
  ```
  Restored to `73d755df…`, sha256 equal before and after.
- **Arm B — a dead pointer, list index.** A link's anchor changed to one no heading slugs to: `…-toolssubpathjss`. Failure, exit 1, naming the link, the anchor and the nearest heading anchor. Restored to `73d755df…`.
- **Arm B2 — a dead pointer in the table index**, so the other file's branch is exercised. Failure in `defect-register.md`, naming its row and nearest anchor. Restored to `ce59a9ff…`.
- **Arm C1 — the index emptied** (44 entries, 0 links): fails on its denominator rather than passing 44 comparisons over nothing — *"yielded no index links, so every link comparison would pass over nothing. An index that names nothing indexes nothing"*. Restored to `73d755df…`.
- **Arm C2 — both files reduced to titles** (0 entries and 0 links, the truly vacuous case): both fail, naming the file and the empty population. Both restored to baseline.
- **The trap, twice.** The worker's own first attempt at arm A was a **silent no-op**: the mutation pattern was a regular expression built from `- [a quotation…]`, where `[...]` is a character class — the same trap that dropped the original index line — and the mutant run came back **green**. Only hashing the artifact before and after caught it, and the driver now mutates line by line and throws on a no-op. A gate written because of that trap found the trap again.
- **What it does not prove**, beyond the bound above: it says nothing about the main tree's uncommitted entries. Run against the shared checkout it reports 16 `gate-proofs` entries and 2 `defect-register` entries with no index line — another session's in-flight additions, reported and not fixed. So the shared tree is red on this gate until those lines exist; the committed tree is green.

## control-chars: the gate chain can run in a linked worktree, and the ignore list still excludes something (`test/control-chars.test.js`)

Taken in the same worktree off `main` at `3d3be2c`. Mutated file restored byte for byte: sha256 `33bb51b4e40c3e83cbe385fc76b966dffb37c49df2574353c23a9970f1a09fd1` before and after (pre-fix bytes `33724d4b987eba3ea9f1ecdac2c5f23f3cd34f5d46d1fb07da91a27463ef9271`).

- **Why it was needed.** The test required every name in the ignore list that exists on disk to be a **directory holding files**. In a linked worktree `.git` is a **file** holding one `gitdir:` line, so the walk found zero files under `.git/` and the assertion fired: `npm run unit` could not pass in a worktree at all — while the fleet constitution makes a worktree the default for any session doing more than a trivial read. Every session was therefore forced into the shared tree, where one session's uncommitted edits mask another's results. This round's own work is the evidence: in a clean worktree the suite is 160/160 and `npm run check` is 10/10 pages, while the same suite in the shared tree was green or red for reasons belonging to whoever else had edited it.
- Claim (the test's own header): the population is the tree, enumerated twice, and the ignored paths really hold files. Bound: it reads the shape of each named path and, for a `.git` file, that it really is a `gitdir:` pointer and that no walked file came from it; it does **not** read what the pointer names, so a worktree whose main repository directory has been deleted still passes.
- **The mutation, and it is the anti-vacuity rule itself.** An empty `progress/` — a name `.gitignore` ignores — added to the loop's list. `npm run unit`, exit 1, 157 tests / 156 pass / 1 fail:
  ```text
  AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: progress/ is on disk and holds no file, so excluding it proves nothing about this walk
      at TestContext.<anonymous> (…/test/control-chars.test.js:413:14)
  ```
  A second mutation — `node_modules` replaced by a file — also goes red: *"node_modules is a file on disk, and only .git has a file shape here"*, so the directory rule was not lost for the other two names. Restored byte for byte, then green.
- **The shape the fix had to respect, found rather than assumed**: the worktree's `node_modules` is a **junction**, which `lstatSync` reports as neither a directory nor a file. A fix written with `lstatSync` would have failed the new shape assertion on `node_modules`; the check uses `statSync` and says why in a comment.
- **What it does not prove**: that the ignore list is complete (it comes from `.gitignore`), nor anything about a path that is neither a directory nor a file — the new branch for that could not be made red here, because Windows offers no cheap FIFO or device node, which the test records rather than implies.

## provenance: a quotation of 通鑑 the page does not mark is invisible, and this is what makes it visible (`test/provenance.test.js`)

Taken on the working tree based on `main` at `0806283`, which also carried a large amount of another session's uncommitted work (biology chapters 4–7 as untracked directories, `tools/*`, and the standing docs). **None of it is touched by this entry**: the five files mutated here are this session's own, and each was restored byte for byte, sha256 recorded below. Captures, UTF-8, under the ignored `out/provproof/`: `green.txt`, `arm-a-red.txt`, `arm-b-red.txt`, `arm-b2-red.txt`, `arm-c1-red.txt`, `arm-c2-red.txt`, `arm-d-red.txt`, `arm-e-red.txt`, `arm-f-red.txt` — the full output of each run, written by the arm itself. They are task evidence and not a proof this repository keeps: what a later reader has is this entry.

- **The defect it was written for, and the fix it landed.** The owner, reading the published second book: *"Just make it perfectly clear what came from the book what didn't it, everywhere."* The book already recorded which run is 資治通鑑's — `lang="zh-Hant"` on every 原文 block and every quotation, held by `test/lexicon.test.js` — but **an attribute is invisible**, and the rule held only where an author had remembered it. Twenty-one runs of 通鑑's own words were printed in 背景 and 思考 as if the book had written them: 「臣光曰」, 「以人事知之」, 「城不浸者三版」, 「三家分智氏之田」, 「智伯之臣」, 「才德兼亡」, 「保障」 among them. One sentence went further and *contradicted the page*: chapter 2's 背景 said of the 前376 entry 「所以这里只转述，不引原文」, while figure 2.1 four lines below quotes it with its citation. The fix is the mark made visible (`--zj-quoted-size` in `--ink-soft`, the treatment `docs/design/tongjian.md` had specified and nothing had implemented), the twenty-one runs marked, that sentence rewritten, and this gate.
- Claim (the test's own header): **every 「…」 run in a chapter page's prose whose text is verbatim 通鑑 sits inside an element carrying `lang="zh-Hant"`**; each `src/figures/zj-*.js` exports `QUOTED_FIELDS` naming exactly the object fields that hold whole 通鑑 句, checked in both directions, and sets `lang: 'zh-Hant'` where it builds the quotation; and `tongjian/index.html` carries the key to the mark in a `<p class="… zj-key …">`.
