- The risk discovery introduces, and why this test derives the answer twice: discovery that quietly finds nothing leaves `PAGES` holding the library and Today, and every page gate goes green having checked no chapter. So the test walks the tree a second time, independently of `discoverBooks`, and requires the two to agree — a check built from the same symbol as the thing it checks proves only that the code agrees with itself. It also asserts the walk itself found something, so the test cannot pass by finding nothing twice.
- Mutation (2026-09-10): the chapter pattern in `discoverBooks` changed from `/^ch(\d{2})-/` to `/^chapter(\d{2})-/`, which matches nothing on this tree. Failure, 3 of 5 tests: `discoverBooks() and a plain walk of the tree disagree about which chapters exist`; `/biology/ch01-what-is-life/ exists on disk but no gate visits it; PAGES is ["/","/today/"]`; and `no book was discovered`. That middle message is the point of the whole test — `shot`, `devices` and `subpath` would have run green over two pages. Restored, `pass 5`.
- What it does not prove: that a discovered page loads, lays out or looks right. That is `shot`, `devices` and a person.

## devices: the phone shapes also run on WebKit and Gecko (`tools/devices.js`)

- Claim (in the tool's own header): the shapes either side of the drawer breakpoint are re-run on WebKit and Firefox over the pages that carry every component, and every check the Chromium arm makes, those arms make too. Bound: **two shapes and two pages** on the secondary engines, against the full nine-device matrix on Chromium; and Firefox cannot emulate a mobile device in Playwright (`isMobile` throws), so its phone arm is a narrow viewport with touch and proves layout differences, not input ones.
- Why it exists: emulating an iPhone in Chromium gives you an iPhone's size, input, user agent and pixel ratio, and Blink's layout. A real iPhone runs WebKit, and all four defects the owner reported were found on a real phone. The three engines were checked by hand during that investigation and the standing gate ran Chromium only, which left the closest available stand-in for the owner's own device outside the chain.
- Mutation (2026-09-11): `<div style="width:2000px;height:2px">` inserted at the top of the library's `<main>`, `DEVICE_PAGES=library DEVICE_ENGINES=webkit DEVICE_ONLY=phone`. Failure: `FAIL webkit phone library (1) / the page scrolls sideways: 2038 px of content in a 390 px viewport`, exit 1. Reverted, `ok`.
- Clean run on the real tree: `DEVICE_PAGES=library,biology/ch01 DEVICE_ENGINES=webkit,firefox` — 8 loads, all `ok`. Chapter 1 holds up on all three engines. (Corrected 2026-09-16: this line quoted `DEVICE_PAGES=library,ch01`, the page id from before ids were book-qualified on 2026-09-12; re-run as quoted, the gate would have dropped the chapter without a word and run the library alone, which is finding 1 of the gates review of 2026-09-16 and the reason `tools/lib/trim.js` now exists. The 8 loads were taken with the old id on the tree of 2026-09-11, where it matched.)
- Also fixed while proving this: the tool's own summary line printed `wanted.length`, the configured device list, not what ran, so a WebKit-only run over two shapes announced `across 9 devices`. It now reports the engine-device pairs and pages it actually loaded. A gate that overstates its own coverage is the same failure as a gate that checks nothing, one step later.
- What it does not prove: anything about how it looks, on any engine. `out/devices/` is for that.

## check: a figure task's expect is one the grader can evaluate (`tools/check-content.js`, `expectProblems` in `src/components/task.js`)

- Claim (in the tool's own header): every `task` item's `expect` parses with the grader's own parser and holds no clause that no figure state can satisfy — a numeric operator against a right-hand side that is not a number. Bound: the grammar, not the figure. A path the figure does not report, or `~` against a field that turns out to be a number, is found only by grading against that figure's describe(), which this check never mounts.
- Why it exists: the grammar's right-hand side is a literal, never a second path, so `heat.comparisonC > heat.waterC` parses and then throws on every grade, for ever. Chapter 2's author wrote exactly that; `npm run check` passed it because it only asked that a task named a figure, and the author found it only by re-running all 26 expectations through the real grader against recorded snapshots. The check imports `parseExpect` from `src/components/task.js` rather than carrying a second parser, because the claim is "the grader can evaluate this" and only the grader can make it. `task.js` gained a Node guard for that (`globalThis.HTMLElement ?? class {}`, and `customElements.define` only where a registry exists) and a `literalFor()` that `compare()` and `expectProblems()` share, so the check and the grade cannot drift.
- Mutation (2026-09-15, on the uncommitted tree holding chapters 1–3): `biology/ch02-chemistry-of-life/items.js` line 582, `heat.comparisonC > 40 and heat.waterC < 36` → `heat.comparisonC > heat.waterC`. Failure, exit 1: `FAIL biology/ch02-chemistry-of-life/index.html (8 figures, 53 glossary entries, 35 objectives, 105 items)` / `biology/ch02-chemistry-of-life/items.js: item "i-specific-heat-2" has an expect no figure state can satisfy: "heat.comparisonC > "heat.waterC"" compares against "heat.waterC", which is not a number; the right-hand side of ">" must be a number such as 40, because an expect compares a field against a literal and never against another field`. Restored byte-identical (sha256 `2d5dc7f6bbb062bc…` before and after).
- Red run, the real one, and not a mutation: the first run of the new check over the tree failed **chapter 1**: `FAIL biology/ch01-what-is-life/index.html (9 figures, 31 glossary entries, 33 objectives, 99 items)` / `biology/ch01-what-is-life/items.js: item "i-resolution-limits-2" has an expect no figure state can satisfy: "nearest === 'influenza' and lensMetres < 2e-7" has trailing "e-7"; join clauses with "and" or "or"`. Chapter 1's items had not changed since 10 September. Asked directly, the grader agreed with the check: its number token was `-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?`, so `2e-7` tokenized as the number `2` and the word `e-7`, `parseExpect` threw, and `<tb-task>` had mounted that item as *broken* for every reader since the day it was written — reported as a `console.warn`, which no gate reads. The grader was the side that was wrong about numbers (`Number('2e-7')` is a number, and `scale` reports `lensMetres` across ten orders of magnitude), so the token now reads an exponent: `-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?(?:[eE][-+]?\d+)?`. An A/B of the two regexes over all 49 real expectations: 48 token streams identical, one differing, `2 | e-7` become `2e-7`. `evalExpect` on the clause afterwards: influenza at 1.0e-7 m → true, at 3.0e-7 m → false, hair at 1.0e-7 m → false. Then `ok   biology/ch01-what-is-life/index.html (… 33 objectives, 99 items)`.
- Fixtures (`test/check-content.test.js`, 38 tests green): the field-against-field clause, checked to name the bank, the item, the clause and what the right side must be; quoted text under `<`; a clause inside `not (… or …)`; three unreadable expressions carrying the parser's own messages; every allowed form passing; and `lensMetres < 2e-7` passing while `2ee` still fails.
- In a browser, because the Node guard would fail silently there (an undefined element, not an error) and no gate mounts this task: a scratch probe standing in for `npm run sitting` and `npm run drive` opened the real chapter-1 page, found `customElements.get('tb-task')` defined and a `<tb-task>` an `HTMLElement`, appended the item's task on `fig-scale`, and read `state: "unanswered"` at mount where the old grammar gave *broken*. Four presses of the figure's own `Next, smaller thing` button took the lens from the animal cell at 2e-5 m to influenza at 1e-7 m; the task's own Check button graded `right`, and the reader saw `That is it. The figure reports nearest = "influenza", lensMetres = 1e-7.` No page error, console error or failed request. `npm run shot` on chapter 1 and Today and `npm run sitting` (2 sittings, 6 answered each, 16 labels read) were green on the same tree.
- What it does not prove: that an expectation is reachable. `x > 5 and x < 3` parses, evaluates, and no state satisfies it; and a bare word with a dot under `===` (`left === heat.waterC`) grades as text and is wrong for ever without a word. Both are named in the handoff of 2026-09-15 and neither is gated.

## check: every item bank is listed on the study page (`tools/check-content.js`, `checkStudySources`)

- Claim (in the tool's own header): every chapter with an `items.js` is listed on every page that carries a `<tb-sitting>`, as an `<a class="tb-source">` whose href resolves to that chapter and whose `data-key` is the chapter id the store files it under (`<book>/chNN`); no two sources share a key; at least one such page exists; and given no bank at all the check fails rather than passing. Bound: one book and one study page — it asks that this page list every bank on disk. Nothing here loads the page: whether a listed bank then loads is `npm run sitting`'s question.
- Why it exists: the site has no build step, so `today/index.html` names its banks by hand, and it named chapter 1 only. Chapter 2 had 105 items across 35 objectives that no sitting could reach, the page still said "33 ideas in this book have a question waiting", and `npm run sitting` passed having never touched a chapter-2 item. It is the same defect as the page list no gate visited: the banks are now found from disk (an `items.js` beside a chapter page) and the rule is keyed on the bank, which is the thing that makes the listing required.
- Mutation (2026-09-15): the chapter-2 line removed from `today/index.html`. Failure, exit 1: `FAIL today/index.html (0 figures, 0 glossary entries, 2 item bank(s) on disk to list)` / `today/index.html: biology/ch02-chemistry-of-life/items.js exists (105 items) and no tb-source in today/index.html points at it, so no sitting can ask one of them; add inside <tb-sitting>: <a class="tb-source" href="../biology/ch02-chemistry-of-life/" data-key="biology/ch02">2 · The chemistry of life</a>`. Restored byte-identical (sha256 `447f514077a5095f…`).
- Clean run after both restores: `ok   today/index.html (0 figures, 0 glossary entries, 2 item bank(s) on disk to list)`, and the tree's only remaining failure is chapter 3's missing `items.js`, which is expected and is not this check's.
- Fixtures (`test/check-content.test.js`): a bank not listed, checked to name the chapter, the file and the exact element; both listed in either order; a wrong `data-key`; a missing `data-key`; two sources sharing a key; no bank given; a page with no `<tb-sitting>`.
- What it does not prove: that Today loads the bank it lists, that the link text matches the chapter's title (the check prints the title it would use and does not require it), or anything about a second book.

## unit: one element table for the chapter-2 molecular figures (`test/element-table.test.js`)

- Claim (in the test's own header): exactly one module under `src/figures/` holds an element table and it is `lib/chem-atoms.js`; `lib/mol-draw.js`'s per-element `fill`, `stroke` and `label` are `var(--…)` of the `token`, `outline` and `label` names that record declares, and every such name is a key of `LIGHT` and `DARK` in `src/palette.js` and a variable in `tokens.css`'s `:root`; the record's element and ion rows equal the colour table in `biology/ch02-chemistry-of-life/FIGURES.md`; chapter 2's eleven modules define `mulberry32` once; and no module under `src/figures/` calls `Math.random()`. Bound: text and exports, never pixels. An element table is an object literal keying three or more of H C N O P S Na Cl K Ca Mg to object values in one file, comments stripped — `foldlab`'s residue classes `H`/`P` and `phlab`'s species `H⁺` key one or two and are not seen, nor is a per-element table of bare numbers such as `bondlab`'s `Z`. The CSS variable name is derived a second way in the test (`inkSoft` → `--ink-soft`), not read from `svg.js`'s `C`, so it is not the code agreeing with itself. The FIGURES.md check reads the `| Thing | Colour |` table by its backticked tokens and skips the bond rows, which live in function defaults. The generator check is bounded to chapter 2: the first draft of this test, run repo-wide, found `mulberry32` defined six times under `src/figures/` (`pond.js`, `pasteur.js`, `lib/cell-common.js`, `lib/cell3-draw.js`, `lib/three-common.js` and `lib/chem-atoms.js`); the other five are other chapters' and merging them is a separate change.
- Why it exists: chapter 2's eight molecular figures were built by two workers in parallel and each wrote a helper with its own element table and its own copy of `mulberry32` — `lib/chem-atoms.js` (physics plus `token`/`outline` palette names; soup, bondlab, water3d, waterprops) and `lib/mol-draw.js` (`fill`/`stroke`/`label` as CSS expressions; phlab, carbonkit, polymer, foldlab). The colours agreed by luck. The covalent radius of chlorine did not: 99 in chem-atoms, which `bondlab`'s `restPm` is built on and its `describe()` reports, against Cordero's 102 in mol-draw, which nothing read. `AGENTS.md`'s one-colour-table invariant covered `tokens.css` against `palette.js` and said nothing about these. `mol-draw` now derives `ELEMENT_CSS` from chem-atoms' record and re-exports its generator, and the record gained the `label` token each atom's symbol is written in (paper, inkSoft on hydrogen) and the three ions FIGURES.md names that only mol-draw carried (K, Ca, Mg), with sourced numbers.
- Mutations (2026-09-16, on the uncommitted tree holding chapters 1–3 and the second book; each helper restored from a snapshot afterwards and checked by sha256 — chem-atoms `41b8ca613f9924c8…`, mol-draw `af59849b25c278b6…` — and the other three tests green under every mutation):
- M1, the literal table mol-draw carried until today pasted back as a local `ATOM_TABLE`. Failure, exit 1: `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: 2 element table(s) under src/figures/ where there must be one, lib/chem-atoms.js:` / `lib/chem-atoms.js (keys H, C, N, O, P, S, Na, K, Ca, Mg, Cl)` / `lib/mol-draw.js (keys C, H, O, N, P, S, Na, K, Ca, Cl, Mg)` / `A second table is a second place the chapter's colours can drift; derive from ELEMENTS in lib/chem-atoms.js instead, as lib/mol-draw.js does`.
- M2, the derivation overridden for one element (`fill: sym === 'O' ? C.gold : cssOf(e.token)`), which M1's scan cannot see because no literal table appears. Failure, exit 1: `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: O: mol-draw fills with var(--gold), chem-atoms declares token coral`.
- M3, the generator's old definition put back in mol-draw in place of the re-export. Failure, exit 1: `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: mulberry32 is defined in lib/chem-atoms.js, lib/mol-draw.js; the one definition is in lib/chem-atoms.js, and every other chapter-2 module imports it`.
- M4, oxygen's token in chem-atoms changed to `gold`, so the two helpers agree with each other and disagree with the brief — the drift M1 and M2 cannot see. Failure, exit 1: `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: O: chem-atoms colours Oxygen gold, FIGURES.md says coral`.
- Clean run after the restores: 4 of 4 green, and `npm run unit` 122 of 122 on the same tree.
- Behaviour held, by the instrument that draws the atoms: `DRIVE_KINDS=soup,bondlab,water3d,waterprops,phlab,carbonkit,polymer,foldlab node tools/drive.js` before the change (45 of 45 steps green) and after it (44 of 45; next bullet), with the before-frames copied out of `out/drive/` first because other workers' runs empty it. The 45 frame pairs compared by bytes, by pixels and by pixel counts within 12 per channel of each light-theme accent: 10 pairs byte-identical (all six `carbonkit`, which draws C, H, N, O, P and S through the derived map; `foldlab-01`; `phlab-04`; `polymer-07`; `polymer-08`), 35 differing, none with an accent that moved by more than a quarter of its own count and 0.2 % of the frame. The large differences are the animated canvas and 3D figures moving on the unpinned clock (`soup` 30–44 % of pixels, `water3d` 7–12 %, `waterprops` up to 11 %, `bondlab` up to 8 %) with coral, water, violet, gold and leaf counts held to within a few pixels; the SVG figures differ by 1–860 px, and the three largest pairs (`polymer-06`, `polymer-03`, `phlab-05`) were looked at side by side at native size: a hover tint on the button the mouse rested on, the Greek α whose fallback font another worker changed between the two runs (`tokens.css` `--font-text`), and antialiasing. Not one atom changed colour.
- The one red step, `water3d pull-a-neighbour-until-it-snaps: pulling one away should not raise the count: 2 -> 3`, passed in the before-run and is a race in the recipe, not a change in the figure: the step reads `bondsHeld` before a click and 400 ms after it and expects no rise, and `bondsHeld` counts neighbours whose `bondPhase(k, time)` is on — each on its own period from the unpinned clock — so a bond can re-form by itself between the two reads. `water3d` imports `hash2, clamp01, lerp, ramp, bondDuty, bondLifePs, phaseOf, formatTempC` from chem-atoms and none of those bodies changed (the diff touches the `ELEMENTS` literal, `atomColours` and comments), and no source hash moved during the run. Not re-run here: the browser gates were budgeted at two runs.
- What it does not prove: that an atom is drawn in the colour the record names — `atom()` and each canvas figure's own drawing code are read by nobody here, and `out/drive/` is the check for the pixels; that the bond colours match the brief; that a table keyed by element names or a numeric per-element table is not a second table; that any physics number is right, since no radius, mass or electronegativity is compared to a source; or anything about the chapter-3 organelle tables, which are keyed by structure and would need a test of their own shape.

## devices: the leftmost header control is in its corner, and the paired book uses a wide screen (`tools/devices.js`)

Taken on the uncommitted round tree of 2026-09-19 — `main` at `f5bd0e9` plus the second book's round (the measure ladder in `tongjian/zj.css`, `src/shell.js` translated to Chinese partway through these runs, figures in flight). This is the proof the 2026-09-19 entry of [defect-register.md](defect-register.md) says was owed: both checks were named there as **added**, not proved, because neither had been made to go red. It has now, three times.

- Claim, in the tool's own header as amended (lines 4–17): the header is there, every control in it fully inside the viewport and at least 24 px on its smallest side, **and the leftmost and the rightmost control each sit on the header's own padding for that side rather than adrift — the LEFT corner and the right corner both**; and **on a chapter of the paired-column book the reading column and its 原文/譯文 block each take at least half of a 1280–1920 px viewport.** Bound (lines 36–46): the corner checks measure the controls that render and compare against the header's own padding with 4 px of tolerance, and a header holding no control is failed above them, so neither can come back green by measuring nothing; the floor is 資治通鑑's chapters alone, at 1280–1920 px, which in the matrix is `desktop` and `desktop-wide` and nothing else, because above 1920 the measure is capped at 63.4rem on purpose and the share falls there by design.
- The corner check as it stands (`tools/devices.js:254-268`): `const leftmost = header.controls.slice().sort((a, b) => a.left - b.left)[0]; if (leftmost) { const gap = leftmost.left - header.headerLeft; const pad = Math.round(parseFloat(header.headerPadLeft) || 0); if (gap > pad + 4) found.push(…) }` — the mirror of the right corner, which reads `width - rightmost.right` against `headerPadRight`. Message: `the leftmost header control "<label>" sits <gap> px from the left edge, where the header's own padding is <pad> px, so it is adrift rather than in the corner`.
- The width check as it stands (`tools/devices.js:436-501`): `PAIRED_BOOKS = ['tongjian']`, `WIDE_FROM = 1280`, `WIDE_TO = 1920`, `MIN_WIDE_SHARE = 50`. On a `tongjian` chapter it measures the reading column (`.tb-text`) and the 原文/譯文 block (`.zj-src`), and fails `the reading column is 698 px of a 1920 px viewport — 36.4%, under the at least 50% of a 1280–1920 px viewport this book is built to use — so the page is not using the width it has`, and the same sentence for `the 原文/譯文 pair of columns`. A page of that book with no `.zj-src`, or with no row carrying both `.zj-pair__src` and `.zj-pair__tr`, fails naming the selector rather than measuring nothing, at every width and not only inside the band. Every page, paired or not, prints the share it measured, so "no floor here" is a number on the line rather than a silence.
- **What each measured before and after.** The corner: before, the leftmost control was measured only for being on screen and 24 px. Measured 2026-09-19 across all nine device shapes, its gap from the header's own left edge is **0 px** — 19.2 px at 320 to 768 px wide, 38.4 px at 1024 and above, against a padding of exactly the same value — so the whole 4 px of tolerance is headroom and nothing is spent on a near miss. The width: the shipped ladder measures 848 px/**66.3%** at 1280, 838.4 px/**58.2%** at 1440 and 1014.4 px/**52.8%** at 1920, identical on ch01, ch02 and ch03, with both boxes equal at every width in the band (`out/widthgate/probe-width.mjs`); the fixed 43.6rem column it replaced was 697.6 px — the 698 px row of `out/width/before.txt` — 55% of 1280, **48% of 1440 and 36% of 1920**, 27% of 2560. The floor is 50%, 2.8 points under the 1920 figure and 13 above the defect, and the margin is small at the wide end because the quantity is CSS arithmetic and not a rendering: `min(100vw - 37.6rem, 63.4rem)` is 52.8% of 1920 whatever the fonts do, so 50% is the statement that the top rung may not fall below 60rem.
- **Mutation 1, the left corner** (`.tb-navtoggle, .tb-header__book { margin-left: 90px !important; }` — the first rendered control is the contents button below 800 px and the book's title above it). Failure, exit 1, on every shape tried, and **the right-corner check stayed green under it**, which is the whole point:
  - `FAIL chromium phone tongjian/ch01 (2): 4 header control(s), the drawer, 4 term(s), 16 prose block(s), 1 wide, reading column 86.9% and pair 83.2% of 390 px, outside the 1280–1920 px band where the floor applies` / `the page scrolls sideways: 413 px of content in a 390 px viewport` / `the leftmost header control "打开本章目录" sits 109 px from the left edge, where the header's own padding is 19 px, so it is adrift rather than in the corner`
  - `FAIL chromium desktop tongjian/ch01 (1): …` / `the leftmost header control "资治通鉴" sits 128 px from the left edge, where the header's own padding is 38 px, so it is adrift rather than in the corner` — and the same line at `desktop-wide`. The label is read off the page, which is why the first run of this mutation, taken before `src/shell.js` was translated, named the same button "Open chapter contents".
- **Mutation 2, the width**, the pre-fix measure put back rather than approximated (`.zj { --measure: 43.6rem !important; --text-width: 43.6rem !important; }`). Failure, exit 1, at both wide shapes, with the edge suite — which a fixed column satisfies perfectly, and did for three days — still green:
  - `FAIL chromium desktop tongjian/ch01 (2): 4 header control(s), the drawer, 4 term(s), 16 prose block(s), 1 wide, reading column 48.4% and pair 48.4% of 1440 px` / `the reading column is 697.6 px of a 1440 px viewport — 48.4%, under the at least 50% of a 1280–1920 px viewport this book is built to use — so the page is not using the width it has` / `the 原文/譯文 pair of columns is 697.6 px of a 1440 px viewport — 48.4%, …`
  - `FAIL chromium desktop-wide tongjian/ch01 (2): … reading column 36.3% and pair 36.3% of 1920 px`, with the same two sentences at `697.6 px` and `36.3%`: the owner's report — 36% — reproduced by the check that is for it, and to the tenth of a point that `out/width/before.txt` recorded.
- **Mutation 3, no subject at all** (`.tb-header button, .tb-header a { display: none !important; }`), because a corner check that finds no leftmost control must fail rather than pass quietly. Failure, exit 1, naming the selector: `FAIL chromium desktop-wide tongjian/ch01 (1): 0 header control(s), the drawer, 4 term(s), 16 prose block(s), 1 wide, reading column 52.8% and pair 52.8% of 1920 px` / `.tb-header holds no button or link, so there is no header control to measure`. That guard is the one that already existed; what mutation 1 adds is that the leftmost check needed no guard of its own to be safe.
- **How the mutations were applied, because none of them is a file edit and the proof has to be reproducible.** A scratch line, `if (process.env.DEVICES_MUTATE) await page.addStyleTag({ content: process.env.DEVICES_MUTATE });`, was added at the gate's own wait point, each CSS string above was passed through it, and the line was then **removed** — `Select-String DEVICES_MUTATE tools/devices.js` matches nothing on the handed-over file. `tongjian/zj.css` belongs to another worker this round and was never written; the injected strings are the pre-fix values themselves, applied to the served page, so what the checks measured is a real layout and not a mock of one.
- Clean runs afterwards, same tree, with the scratch hook gone (`Select-String DEVICES_MUTATE tools/devices.js` matches nothing). `DEVICE_ONLY=phone,desktop,desktop-wide DEVICE_PAGES=tongjian/ch01`: 3 loads, all `ok`, printing `reading column 58.2% and pair 58.2% of 1440 px` and `reading column 52.8% and pair 52.8% of 1920 px`. Then the whole device list on the one paired chapter, `DEVICE_PAGES=tongjian/ch01`: **9 loads over 9 engine-device pairs, all `ok`**, 320 px to 1920, the two wide shapes carrying the floor and the other seven saying `outside the 1280–1920 px band where the floor applies`. Then `DEVICE_PAGES=tongjian/ch01,biology/ch01 DEVICE_ONLY=phone,desktop,desktop-wide`: 10 loads over 7 engine-device pairs on Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, with the biology chapter reading `reading column 36.3% of 1920 px, no paired columns on this page, no floor outside the paired book` — the scope decision, printed by the gate rather than asserted. The full nine-device matrix over every page is the owner's to run and was not run here; a transient `zj-split.js did not load (Unexpected identifier 'npm')` appeared in one of these runs and was gone in the next, which is another worker's file mid-write and not this change.
- **Frames looked at**, because the numbers are not the look: `out/widthgate/header-phone-small-crop.png` (320×78 at 1:1) shows the round ☰ contents button flush with the paper's left margin, the same distance in from the left edge as the moon toggle's disc is from the right, with 资治通鉴 and 今日 between them; `out/widthgate/header-desktop-wide-crop.png` (1920×78 at 1:1) shows 资治通鉴 starting on the header's own 38.4 px padding at the far left, 书库 / 卷一 周纪一 beside it, an empty middle, and 今日 and the toggle at the far right. The full frames (`header-phone-small-full.png`, `header-desktop-wide-full.png`) show the same header over the chapter opener on the phone, and over the rail, the reading column and the margin column at 1920.
- **What the width floor deliberately does not cover, measured rather than assumed.** A biology chapter holds no `.zj-pair`, and its column is a fixed 698 px — 54.5%, 48.4%, **36.3%** and 27.3% of 1280, 1440, 1920 and 2560 — which is the shape this check was built to catch. The library, the two book pages and Today have no `.tb-text` at all. The floor is the paired book's claim; imposing it on the other book would be this gate inventing a design decision nobody has taken, and that is stated in the header rather than left to be discovered.
- What it does not prove. The corner checks prove where the leftmost and the rightmost boxes sit, **not that the control works** — a dead button in its corner passes them, and the drawer suite is what presses it. They measure what renders: an element with no box is skipped, and mutation 3 is what turns an empty header red. The width floor proves a share of the viewport, not a good measure: measured at 1920 px on ch02, a full prose line runs **52–56 Han characters** (`out/widthgate/probe-prose.mjs`), past the ceiling of 40, where `docs/work/4_zizhi-tongjian/restructure.md` records the accepted trade as **43.1** — that number was taken on an earlier state of the ladder, and the shipped rungs are wider than the paragraph describes. That is a question for that document's owner; a line-length check here would contradict the decision it records, which is why the floor counts viewport share. *[Corrected in the note below, same day: the question was answered by capping the line rather than by moving a rung, so the tree this bullet describes is one revision old — the 52–56 stands as what that instrument measured, and it is a count of every non-space glyph where the 45 Han characters of the same lines are what the band is stated in.]* Above 1920 px nothing here applies, and no device in the matrix sits between 800 and 1024 px, where the drawer becomes the rail.
- **Added 2026-09-19: the measure this entry reports was retuned an hour later, and the 52–56 above is a record of the state it was taken on rather than of the tree now.** The rungs were left where they are and the prose was capped inside them — `--prose-measure: 50rem` in §6b of `tongjian/zj.css` — so a full prose line now sets **36 Han characters on chapter 1 and 35 on chapters 2 and 3, with the worst line 38, the same at 1280, 1440, 1600, 1920 and 2560 px**, while the reading column and the pair stay untouched at 848 / 838.4 / 998.4 / 1014.4 px, which is why the floor above and its 52.8% at 1920 still hold. The instrument is `out/remeasure/probe-prose-han.mjs` — Han characters alone, every full line of every prose paragraph, then that paragraph's median — and it returned the same numbers on all four revisions of `tongjian/zj.css` it was run against while this note was being written (sha256 `D65E6909…`, `B0C9871F…`, `DFC92625…`, `7539F29D…`), with the last two runs comparing that hash on both sides of each of their 15 loads and never seeing it move. **The 52–56 was two things at once**: a real measurement of the shipped ladder, and a count of every non-space glyph where the design's band is stated in Han characters — `out/widthgate/probe-prose.mjs` counts Latin, digits and CJK punctuation as well. Counted for Han alone, those uncapped lines hold **45 characters at 1920 px**, past the ceiling of 40, so the retune was owed on either count. Two controls on the served page, injected and never written into `zj.css` (`out/remeasure/probe-cap-control.mjs`): `--prose-measure: 43.6rem` gives 31 Han characters at 1920 px, and `--prose-measure: 63.4rem` gives 45 Han with the old 52–56 back as its slot count (`out/remeasure/prose-han-control-436.json`, `…-uncapped.json`). What the floor proves is unchanged — a share of the viewport, not a good measure — and the line-length question it declines to judge is settled at 36/35/35 by the cap, not by the floor.

## shot: a figure's arithmetic reaching the DOM as a negative or as NaN (`tools/shot.js`, `auditFigureGeometry`)

Taken 2026-09-16 on `main` at `f5bd0e9` plus a working tree another worker was writing at the same time (the 資治通鑑 colour round: `tongjian/*`, `src/shell.js`, `tools/devices.js`, and the colour-hook block of `tools/shot.js` itself). Nothing of theirs was touched; `src/figures/cytoskeleton.js` carried no change but this one, so each mutation below is a one-file change against `f5bd0e9` and `git checkout HEAD -- src/figures/cytoskeleton.js` restores the defect exactly rather than approximately.

The defect this gates. Chapter 3's figure 3.6 drew its tubulin lattice with `width: ((s1 - s0) * k - 0.7)`, a 0.7 px hairline subtracted from a dimer's span converted from nanometres at `k` px/nm. The guard above it was in nanometres alone (`if (s1 - s0 < 1.2) continue`) and could not see the hairline. At a tablet the figure takes its narrow composition and the track is 224 px for 400 nm, so `k = 0.36`: the 1.8 nm remnant at the plus end of the third row is 0.65 px, and `0.65 − 0.7` is `width="-0.1"`. The row height `(2r/rows − 0.7)` has the same shape and goes negative on the mount-time paint, before the stage has been measured. On `main` this was logging `<rect> attribute width: A negative value is not valid.` on the live site, and failing three of shot's sixty loads, two of devices' ninety-eight and one of subpath's forty-four.

- Claim, in the tool's own header (lines 9–12 and 37–41): a page fails when any geometric attribute a mounted figure wrote into the settled DOM is negative where SVG forbids it, or is not a finite number, naming the page, viewport, theme, figure kind, element and attribute.
- Bound, stated there and load-bearing here: it reads the DOM **as it stands once the page has settled**, so a bad value in a frame already replaced belongs to the console-error check above it and not to this one — the two together are what covers the class. It asks whether a value is a number of the sign SVG allows, never whether it is the right number. It sees only figures a page mounts (a registered kind no chapter uses yet is `npm run drive`'s and `npm run narrow`'s), and only authored SVG: a canvas figure, a WebGL figure and 字与词 write none, which is why every line prints the count — `0 SVG attr(s)` is a scan that compared nothing, and a page that moved a figure into a shadow root fails naming it rather than scanning an empty subtree.

- **Mutation 1, the defect itself**, restored verbatim with `git checkout HEAD -- src/figures/cytoskeleton.js` and confirmed present (`src/figures/cytoskeleton.js:181` of that revision reads `width: ((s1 - s0) * k - 0.7).toFixed(1), height: (hgt - 0.7).toFixed(1)`). `SHOT_PAGES=biology/ch03 SHOT_VIEWPORTS=tablet SHOT_THEMES=light node tools/shot.js` — failure, exit 1:
  - `FAIL: 1 of 1 page loads had problems; see out/shots/report.json`
  - `biology/ch03 tablet light: cytoskeleton (fig-cytoskeleton) <rect> inside .cy-fil has width="-0.1", and -0.1 is negative, which SVG does not allow for width. A figure writes its shapes from arithmetic, so this is a number that came out wrong upstream — work out why the value inverts or goes undefined rather than clamping it here; what would satisfy this is width being a finite number that is not negative, or the shape not being drawn at all.`
  - The run's own account of what it read: `{"found":1,"elements":789,"attributes":2960,"kinds":["symbiont","cytoskeleton","cilium","plantcell3d"]}` — so the scan reached the figure that holds the defect, and `found: 1` is the bound above being true rather than being claimed: the eleven other negatives the same run logged as console errors (`-0.5`, `-0.6`) are the mount-time paint, already replaced by the time the DOM is read.
- **Mutation 2, the half no existing check could see.** `'stroke-width': w.toFixed(1)` → `'stroke-width': (w * undefined).toFixed(1)` in `buildIntermediates`, on the FIXED figure, so the only defect in the tree is this one. A negative or `NaN` presentation value is parsed as CSS, dropped in silence and never logged; the stroke simply renders at 1 px. Same command — failure, exit 1, with **no `console.error` line anywhere in the output**:
  - `biology/ch03 tablet light: cytoskeleton (fig-cytoskeleton) <path> inside .cy-if has stroke-width="NaN", and "NaN" is not a number. A figure writes its shapes from arithmetic, so this is a number that came out wrong upstream — work out why the value inverts or goes undefined rather than clamping it here; what would satisfy this is stroke-width being a finite number that is not negative, or the shape not being drawn at all.` (×3, one per intermediate filament)
  - This is the mutation that makes the check worth having. Mutation 1 turns the gate red twice over, once through the console and once through the scan; mutation 2 turns it red only through the scan.
- A first attempt at mutation 1 was a **partial no-op** and is recorded because it nearly became the proof: a scripted three-part replacement matched only the one pattern that contained no newline, because the patterns carried CRLF and the file is LF. The script's `if (s === before)` guard passed — something had changed — while two thirds of the defect was never reintroduced. It was caught by reading the file the edit should have changed rather than the tool's report of it, which is why the mutation used here is a `git checkout` of the whole file.
- Clean run afterwards, the fix restored from a byte-for-byte snapshot: `SHOT_PAGES=biology/ch03 node tools/shot.js` — **6 of 6 loads `ok`**, `2353 SVG attr(s) over 636 element(s) in 4 figure(s)` at phone, `2955 over 788` at tablet, `3930 over 992` at desktop, in both themes.
- **The fix did not delete the drawing**, measured rather than eyeballed (`tools/inspect.js` is the instrument for the frame; this counts what is behind it). Microtubule rects in the settled DOM, before → after: phone (track 165×425, narrow) 254 → 254, min width 0.90 px; tablet (287×224, narrow) 254 → **253**, min width −0.10 → **0.30**; desktop (665×366, wide) 379 → 379, min width 1.00. One tile of 254 is no longer drawn, at the far plus end of one of five rows, and it was 0.65 px wide. The phone escapes the defect for the reason the fix names: its track is 425 px for 400 nm, so `k = 0.86` and the same remnant is 1.55 px.
- **Frames looked at**, four, at native size: `out/inspect-cy-tablet-light`, `-tablet-dark`, `-desktop-light`, `-desktop-dark`, each `biology-ch03-<viewport>-<theme>-00-fig-cytoskeleton.png` from `tools/inspect.js`. At tablet the narrow composition still draws the microtubule as a segmented column with its lattice, the actin filament, the intermediate filaments behind, both ends marked and the cargo on the track; at desktop the wide composition draws the full horizontal lattice in alternating α/β tiles across all five rows. Both themes.
- **It was red on a page with nothing wrong with it, and that is in here because it is the way a check of this shape fails.** The first version read `<marker orient="auto-start-reverse">` on chapter 1's homeostasis figure as "not a number" and failed `biology/ch01` at all three widths — a legal SVG keyword the word list did not carry. Found by running the check over every page of both books before landing it, not by reading the specification: `SHOT_PAGES=library,biology,biology/ch01,biology/ch02,biology/ch03,tongjian,tongjian/ch01,tongjian/ch02,tongjian/ch03,today node tools/shot.js`. Fixed by adding `auto-start-reverse` and `auto-reverse` to the keywords and the angle and time units to the number pattern, and re-run: **60 loads over ten pages, three widths, both themes, and not one geometry problem**, with `10129 SVG attr(s) over 3790 element(s) in 8 figure(s)` on chapter 1 alone. The twelve loads still failing in that run are the 資治通鑑 worker's own new font check on `library` and `tongjian/ch02` (`… are drawn by Noto Sans SC, which this page never loaded`), and `biology/ch04` was left out because it is an untracked half-written chapter whose eight figure kinds are not registered yet — `npm run check` is red on it for that reason and was before this change.
- What it does not prove. Nothing about whether a number is the RIGHT number: a rect at the wrong place, the wrong size or the wrong colour passes. Nothing about attributes outside the two lists and the six built strings in `auditFigureGeometry` — `mask`, `clip-path`, `filter` and anything set through `style` or CSS are unread. Nothing about a value that is negative for one frame and fixed by the next, which is the console-error check's half. Nothing about canvas or WebGL figures, which write no attributes to read. And nothing about a figure at a width outside shot's three: the defect was found at 1024 and 1440 and is absent at 390, so a fourth width could hold another one — the scan runs wherever shot's matrix runs, and no wider.

## shot: no colour hook paints nothing, and no marked target is left without one (`tools/shot.js`)

- **Taken on the uncommitted round tree of 2026-09-19 — `main` at `f5bd0e9` plus the second book's round** (`tools/shot.js`, `tongjian/zj.css` and `tools/devices.js` modified in the working tree; `test/meta.test.js` and `test/strings.test.js` untracked). The evidence is under the ignored `out/shotgate/`: `mutation1-final.txt`, `mutation2-final.txt` and `mutation3-final.txt` (the three mutations), `final-run.txt` (the real defect the check caught), `final-run3.txt` (the clean trimmed run), and `probe-hooks.mjs`, the scratch probe the check was designed against.
- **The defect it was built on, in the tool's own words (the comment above `HOOK_CENSUS`).** *"`.zj [data-state="wei"]` matched its element and did not paint it: chapter 1's 字 glyphs are `.zj .zj-lex__glyph { color: var(--ink) }`, the same specificity (0,2,0) stated 90 lines later, so the later rule won and the glyph stayed ink. `el.matches('.zj [data-state="wei"]')` was true while the computed colour was #1d1a17. `npm run check` reads authored HTML and cannot see a cascade; a screenshot shows a colour on the elements that work and nothing suspicious on the ones that do not; and every check above this one in this file is about console errors, figures, overflow and caption words. A rule that silently loses the cascade and a rule that was never written are the same pixels."* What closes it is the second `[data-state]` on each house rule — one more unit of specificity and nothing else — at `tongjian/zj.css:949`, `:953`, `:957` and `:961` when this entry was written, `:1045`, `:1049`, `:1053` and `:1057` after the later retune of that file, each carrying the measurement in the comment above it.
- Claim (in the tool's own header, lines 4–12): a page passes when it reaches the handshake with no console error, no uncaught page error, no failed request, no figure in the error state, no horizontal overflow of the document, **no colour hook that paints nothing and no marked target no hook reaches** — *"(a page that must carry a hook and has none, a rule that matches its element and loses the cascade, a hook with nothing to paint, a target of a hook's rule with no hook above it, or a page that rebuilds while an attribute is off — see the hook block below)"* — no geometric attribute in a mounted figure's SVG that is negative where SVG forbids it or is not a finite number, and, on a page whose `<html lang>` this gate knows the script of, every figure caption's number word (`.fig-num`) and the figure's aria-label in that script's word. Fails otherwise and names the page, viewport, theme and the errors. **This entry proves the colour-hook half alone**: the geometry check the same header carries has its own entry in this file (`auditFigureGeometry`) and the caption-word check has no red proof here — it is owed, and nothing in this entry stands in for it.
- **Correction, 2026-09-19: the caption-word check is no longer owed one, and it now has its first red proof in this repository.** `FIGURE_WORDS['zh-hans']` pointed at the Traditional word (`src/components/figure.js:82`, one character — the exact defect `0482dde` fixed) fails `SHOT_PAGES=tongjian/ch01 SHOT_VIEWPORTS=desktop SHOT_THEMES=light node tools/shot.js`, exit 1, on both things the check reads: `the caption of fig-split numbers itself "圖 1.1", but a page in lang="zh-Hans" writes its figure word as "图"` and `the aria-label of fig-split is "圖 1.1: 三家分晋：从灭智到命侯", so a screen reader hears a word other than "图" before it on a lang="zh-Hans" page` (`out/proofs/check3-shot-red.utf8.txt`), with `src/components/figure.js` restored byte for byte to sha256 `065BEE44…`. The proof is not circular, and the tool's own comment is the reason: `tools/shot.js` holds the gate's own `figureWordFor(lang)`, with its own keying and its own fallbacks, which is a separate derivation from the frame's `FIGURE_WORDS` — the gate is not reading the code's answer back to it. The control run on the unmutated tree prints the denominator rather than a silence: `ok tongjian/ch01 desktop light (… 1 caption(s) in "图" …)`. **The bound, measured**: *dropping* the `zh-hans` key instead of corrupting it leaves `npm run shot` **green**, because the base-language fallback yields the very word the gate expects, so this check fails a **wrong word** and cannot fail a **missing language key whose fallback agrees**; `test/strings.test.js` is what catches that one (`FIGURE_WORDS has no zh-hans entry, so a page declaring lang="zh-hans" falls through to another script's word for the same figure`, `out/proofs/check3b-fallback-strings-red.utf8.txt`).
- Bound, as the header states it (lines 14–16): *"three viewports, two themes, one pinned time, one renderer (SwiftShader unless SHOT_GPU=1); SHOT_PAGES, SHOT_VIEWPORTS and SHOT_THEMES trim the matrix and a trimmed run proves only its part, and a value naming no page, viewport or theme stops the run rather than emptying it (tools/lib/trim.js)."*
- Bound, the colour-hook half (lines 19–36, quoted): *"The caption-word check knows English and Chinese (`en`, `zh`, `zh-Hans`, `zh-Hant`) and nothing else; a page in another language is not asked. The colour-hook check reads its hooks from the stylesheets the page loads and only from rules scoped to the book that declare a colour property; it proves the attribute changes a computed colour, not that the colour is the right one or a readable one (out/colour2/contrast.mjs measures contrast), not that it survives a hover or a pressed state, and not that a page's JavaScript paints anything — `el.style.color = …`, a canvas and an SVG fill attribute are outside it entirely. A hook whose only rule sits inside a media block that excludes one of the three widths fails at that width, which is intended for a mark that must be there and wrong for one the design means to drop; a rule inside a block that does not apply is reported as such rather than as a cascade loss. The half that catches an unreached target derives its target from the rule text: the rules `<hook="value"> <subject>` are read together as "every `<subject>` in this book sits under an element carrying the hook attribute, with one of the values those rules spend". The values have to be read together, because the figure's houses are three rules over one shared `.zjs-house-char` class and each char is reached by its own house's rule and not the other two. That is still a claim about CSS that is not true in general: a rule scoping a shared class to one value while the other values' elements are meant to stay unpainted (`.zj [data-state="wei"] .zj-lex__pinyin`) fails this check until a rule exists for each value or the subject is narrowed to what it means. A selector reaching its subject through a sibling combinator is skipped rather than guessed at."*
- The required-hook half, and why it cannot come back green by measuring nothing (header, in the block above `HOOK_CENSUS` — the line numbers this entry used to cite moved with the block, so it is named rather than numbered): `HOOK_CENSUS` is a per-page table — ch01 `[data-state="wei"]×2 [data-state="zhao"]×2 [data-state="han"]×2 [data-date]×2`; ch02 six selectors; ch03 two — built from the chapter's own rendered pages, and *"The table asks for a FLOOR per hook, not for presence and not for an exact count. … An exact count would fail a chapter that ADDS a 魏 mark, so the check tests `found < floor` and nothing above it: one mark more is free, one mark fewer is not. … Its selectors are measured even when no rule is found for them, so a DELETED rule — the one failure a discovery by rule cannot see — fails too."* **The sentence this entry used to carry here — that the table asked for presence and not for a count, so a chapter that loses one of three 魏 mentions passes — described the check before its floor landed, and is wrong about the shipped tool**: the check fails a count that drops while staying above zero. The floor, its two mutations and its live loose end are the `shot` entry *a page may not carry fewer colour hooks than its census says*. The hooks themselves are not a list: they are read out of the stylesheets a page loads, so a new rule scoped under `.zj`/`.tb-zj…` that declares a colour from a `data-*` attribute is covered the moment it exists, which is how the figure hook `.tb-zjs .zjs-house[data-house="wei"] .zjs-house-char` came under the check without a line being added for it. **What that census holds for ch02 changed twice while this entry was being written**: the ch02 row still reads six selectors, and its `[data-date]` floor read 4 against a page that had come to carry 6, so that hook's count could have halved without the check saying so — the floor was raised to 6 by the worker holding `tools/shot.js`, and the `shot` entry *a page may not carry fewer colour hooks than its census says* carries the numbers and the captures on both sides of that change.
- **Mutation 1, the cascade-loss half** (`.zj [data-state="wei"][data-state]` → `.zj [data-state="wei"]` in `tongjian/zj.css:949`, the specificity device removed and nothing else changed; at `:1045` on the file as it stands now). Failure, from `out/shotgate/mutation1-final.txt`, the run's own tail reading `FAIL: 1 of 1 page loads had problems; see out/shots/report.json`:
  `FAIL tongjian/ch01 desktop light (2046 ms)`
  `  tongjian/ch01 desktop: the colour hook data-state="wei" on <tb-char.zj-lex__glyph in #notes> "魏" painted nothing — ".zj [data-state="wei"]" matches <tb-char.zj-lex__glyph in #notes> "魏", and removing the attribute changed no computed colour on it or on anything inside it, so a later rule or a heavier one is winning and the mark is missing exactly as it would be if this rule did not exist. What would satisfy this: one more unit of specificity in that rule, or stating it after the rule that beats it.`
- **Mutation 2, the hook whose rule no longer names its element** (both `.zj .tb-margin-note[data-note="textual"]` rules at `tongjian/zj.css:894` and `:902` renamed to `.tb-margin-note-none`; `:990` and `:998` now). Failure, from `out/shotgate/mutation2-final.txt`:
  `FAIL tongjian/ch02 desktop light (3598 ms)`
  `  tongjian/ch02 desktop: the colour hook data-note="textual" on <aside.tb-margin-note in #source> "异文：「蜹」是来源整理本的写" painted nothing, and the rule that spends it names no element here — ".zj .tb-margin-note-none[data-note="textual"]" has the subject ".tb-margin-note-none[data-note="textual"]", which matches nothing on or inside this element. Either the rule stopped matching the element the attribute is on, or the element holds nothing for it to paint; its subject is what the element must contain.`
  and the same line for the second note in that section, `"异文：这一句的底本作「蔡、皋"`.
- **Mutation 3, the unreached-target half** (the rule at `tongjian/zj.css:866`, `.zj [data-date] .zj-yr,` — at `:962` now — extended with a second selector `.zj [data-date] .zj-src,` — a target with no hook above it, which the cascade half cannot see). Failure, from `out/shotgate/mutation3-final.txt`:
  `FAIL tongjian/ch02 desktop light (6239 ms)`
  `  tongjian/ch02 desktop: .zj-src is a target of ".zj [data-date] .zj-src" and no data-date reaches it — <ol.zj-src in #source> "初，智宣子將以瑤為後。 当初" takes that rule's colour only when an ancestor carries [data-date], and without one it keeps the colour of the prose around it, so the mark the rule was written for is absent. What would satisfy this: put [data-date] on the element that holds it, or take the mark off this element.`
  and the same line for the two other `.zj-src` blocks in that section.
- **Red run, the real one, and not a mutation** (`out/shotgate/final-run.txt`): four of sixteen loads failed, all of them chapter 1 — `FAIL: 4 of 16 page loads had problems; see out/shots/report.json`. The check reported the mark missing on a page that must carry the hook:
  `FAIL tongjian/ch01 phone light (1326 ms)`
  `  tongjian/ch01 phone: the colour hook data-date on <p in #history> "一句话，一件事：周天子把晋国" painted nothing, and the rule that spends it names no element here — ".zj [data-date] .zj-yr" has the subject ".zj-yr", which matches nothing on or inside this element. Either the rule stopped matching the element the attribute is on, or the element holds nothing for it to paint; its subject is what the element must contain.`
  `  tongjian/ch01 phone: .zj-yr is a target of ".zj [data-date] .zj-yr" and no data-date reaches it — <span.zj-yr in #history> "前四〇三" takes that rule's colour only when a [data-date] ancestor carries the hook, and without one it keeps the colour of the prose around it, so the mark the rule was written for is absent. What would satisfy this: put data-date on the element that holds it, or take the mark off this element.`
  `  tongjian/ch01 phone: .zjs-house-char is a target of ".tb-zjs .zjs-house[data-house="wei"] .zjs-house-char" and no data-house="wei" reaches it — <span.zjs-house-char in #history> "赵" takes that rule's colour only when a [data-house="wei"] ancestor carries the hook, and without one it keeps the colour of the prose around it, so the mark the rule was written for is absent. What would satisfy this: put data-house="wei" on the element that holds it, or take the mark off this element.`
  Each of chapter 1's four loads carries the same ten failures: the unreached `[data-date]` hook, three unreached `.zj-yr` targets and six unreached `.zjs-house-char` targets across the three houses. The chapter is `ok` in the later clean run — `11 colour-hook mark(s) painted, 7 hooked target(s) reached` — and `tongjian/zj.css:866` as handed over carries the rule with `.zj-yr` alone.
- Clean run, same tree, trimmed to the four 資治通鑑 pages at two widths in both themes (`out/shotgate/final-run3.txt`), 16 loads:
  `ok   tongjian/ch01 desktop light (1279 ms, 1 figures ready, 1 caption(s) in "图", 11 colour-hook mark(s) painted, 7 hooked target(s) reached, 0 SVG attr(s) over 0 element(s) in 0 figure(s))`
  `ok   tongjian/ch02 desktop light (6570 ms, 1 figures ready, 1 caption(s) in "图", 29 colour-hook mark(s) painted, 10 hooked target(s) reached, 0 SVG attr(s) over 0 element(s) in 0 figure(s))`
  `ok   tongjian/ch03 desktop light (5734 ms, 1 figures ready, 1 caption(s) in "图", 2 colour-hook mark(s) painted, 0 SVG attr(s) over 0 element(s) in 0 figure(s))`
  `shot: 16 page loads clean; screenshots in out/shots/`
  The book's own landing page prints `no colour hooks`, which is the table saying that page declares none and is not asked.
- **How the mutations were applied.** The captures record the runs, not the edit: the three mutations are the three one-line edits to `tongjian/zj.css` named above, and the tree as handed over carries the unmutated text — the specificity device at `:949`, the two `[data-note="textual"]` rules at `:894` and `:902`, and `.zj [data-date] .zj-yr,` at `:866` with no `.zj-src` beside it — `:1045`, `:990`, `:998` and `:962` in that order on the file as it stands now, since the later retune moved every one of them down the file by the same 96 lines. Unlike the `devices` entry's mutations, no scratch hook is recorded here, so a rerun of these three means making those three edits and undoing them.
- What it does not prove. The attribute changes a computed colour; **not** that the colour is the right one or a readable one (`out/colour2/contrast.mjs` is the measure for that, and the header says so). Not that the mark survives a hover or a pressed state. Not that a page's JavaScript paints anything: `el.style.color = …`, a canvas fill and an SVG `fill` attribute are outside it entirely. A hook whose only rule sits in a media block that excludes one of the three widths fails by design, which is right for a mark that must be there and wrong for one the design means to drop; a rule inside a block that does not apply is reported as such rather than as a cascade loss. The unreached-target half is a claim about CSS that is not true in general, and the header names the case: a rule that scopes a shared class to one value while the other values' elements are meant to stay unpainted fails until a rule exists for each value or the subject is narrowed. A selector reaching its subject through a sibling combinator is skipped rather than guessed at.

## unit: no authored page writes markup into a `content=` attribute (`test/meta.test.js`)

- **The defect, and why only a parser sees it** (header, lines 7–15, quoted): *"Why that is a defect and not a style rule: an attribute value ends at its closing quote. A tag written inside one does not disappear — that quote closes the ATTRIBUTE instead, the rest of the value is re-read as if the author had typed it after the tag, and it arrives in the document as text and as further attributes. `tongjian/ch03-cai-de-lun/index.html` carried `content="…臣光曰「<span lang="zh-Hant">智伯之亡也，才勝德也</span>」。…"`, and the parser gave the reader a stray line above the book's own opening line, a description truncated to `…「<span lang=`, and the page's own `<script type="module">` moved out of `<head>` into `<body>`. `npm run check` passed the page, because a checker reading the authored text sees a well-formed string; only a parser sees this damage. The fix removed the two tags and kept the sentence, which is plain text."* And the class: *"The class is "a `content=` attribute holds markup", not "this one attribute": every page and every `content=` attribute on it is read, and the failure names the page, the line, the tag, the attribute's value and the sequence in it."*
- Claim (in the test's own header, lines 3–5): *"It proves that no `content=` attribute in the authored markup of a page the gates visit holds a tag-looking sequence — a `<` followed by an ASCII letter or `/`, which is the only way the content checker's tokenizer opens a tag (`<([a-zA-Z][\w-]*)` and `</([a-zA-Z][\w-]*)` in tools/check-content.js)."*
- Bound (header, lines 21–38, quoted): *"The files it reads are the pages `tools/lib/browser.js` discovers, on disk, as text: the library (`index.html`), each book's landing page, every `<book>/chNN-*/index.html`, and Today. `lab/` is not in that list and is not read here either, and a page shape the tokenizer never matches is invisible to this file for the same reason it is invisible to `npm run check`. … Nothing here renders, and a `content=` value assembled at runtime — a template literal, a string built from data, `setAttribute('content', …)` — cannot be seen by a file-reading gate at all. A tag built that way is this check's stated blind spot, and no header comment can close it: `npm run shot` is the instrument for the damage once it happens, because it loads every page in chromium and fails on the console error a script moved out of `<head>` raises. … It says nothing about what a description should SAY, and nothing about its script. A Traditional quotation inside 「」 in a `content=` attribute is read by no gate: `test/lexicon.test.js` blanks every 「」 run before it looks for a Traditional-only character, and `test/strings.test.js` reads the attributes a reader is *told* (`aria-label`, `title`, `alt`, …), which `content=` is not. Only the shape of the value is checked here. … A page with no `content=` attribute is evidence of nothing, so the page count and the attribute count are asserted non-zero and reported, and the rule is exercised on a fixture built from the defect and on two values that must not fire, so "did not run" cannot read as "passed"."*
- **Mutation, the defect put back**: `<span lang="zh-Hant">…</span>` restored around the quotation in `tongjian/ch03-cai-de-lun/index.html:7`, which is the exact text the fix removed. Failure, from `out/meta/red.txt` — the run's own summary reading `ℹ tests 2 / ℹ pass 1 / ℹ fail 1`, with the fixture test `the rule fires on the defect, and only on it` green beside it:
  `✖ no authored page writes markup into a content= attribute (411.7381ms)`
  `ℹ pages: 11 read (library, biology, biology/ch01, biology/ch02, biology/ch03, biology/ch04, tongjian, tongjian/ch01, tongjian/ch02, tongjian/ch03, today); content= attributes checked: 22; holding a tag-looking sequence: 1`
  `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: 1 content= attribute(s) hold markup where the parser reads plain text. An attribute value ends at its closing quote, so the tag does not stay inside it: it closes the attribute early, and every character after it lands in the page as ordinary text and as further attributes — which is how the ch03 description put a stray line above the book's own opening line and moved its <script> into <body>. Write the value as plain text; if a literal less-than sign is meant, escape it as &lt;, which is text and not a tag. A meta description is plain text, so a <span lang="zh-Hant"> marking a quotation Traditional cannot live in one — keep the 「…」 run and drop the tags.`
  `    tongjian/ch03-cai-de-lun/index.html:7 — <meta content="…"> holds 「<span lang=」; the value reads 「《资治通鉴》卷一 周纪一：臣光曰「<span lang=」`
  `  1 !== 0`
- Clean run, same tree after the restore, the whole unit suite (`out/meta/unit.txt`):
  `✔ no authored page writes markup into a content= attribute (128.5732ms)`
  `ℹ pages: 11 read (library, biology, biology/ch01, biology/ch02, biology/ch03, biology/ch04, tongjian, tongjian/ch01, tongjian/ch02, tongjian/ch03, today); content= attributes checked: 22; holding a tag-looking sequence: 0`
  `ℹ tests 140 / ℹ pass 140 / ℹ fail 0`
  The denominators are the same 11 pages and 22 attributes as in the red run, so the red run and the clean run compared the same population.
- The restore in file form (`out/meta/ch03-final.diff`), which is also what shows the defect is out of the tree: the value went from `content="《资治通鉴》卷一 周纪一：臣光曰「<span lang="zh-Hant">智伯之亡也，才勝德也</span>」。原文逐字可点，附现代汉语翻译、字词卡与背景。"` to `content="《资治通鉴》卷一 周纪一：臣光曰「智伯之亡也，才勝德也」。原文逐字可点，附现代汉语翻译、字词卡与背景。"`; line 7 of the page as handed over is the second of those. The same diff carries two other workers' changes to that file (`<html … class="zj">` and `data-note="textual"` on a margin note), so it is not this check's diff alone.
- What it does not prove. A `content=` value assembled at runtime — a template literal, a string built from data, `setAttribute('content', …)` — is its stated blind spot, and no file-reading gate can see it; `npm run shot` is the instrument for the damage once it happens. It says nothing about what a description should say, and nothing about its script: a Traditional quotation inside 「」 in a `content=` attribute is read by no gate, because `test/lexicon.test.js` blanks 「」 runs and `test/strings.test.js` reads the attributes a reader is told, which `content=` is not. `lab/` is outside the page list and is not read. And a page shape the content checker's tokenizer never matches is invisible to this file too, since it reads the page through that same tokenizer.

## strings: a Chinese page's chrome is Chinese, and in the page's own script (`test/strings.test.js`)

Written by the worker who wrote the checks; the failing output below is the one they captured, carried in `out/sortstring/gate-proof-text.md` (the census's own output is `out/sortstring/census-report.txt`, and the round's probes are `out/sortstring/probe.mjs`, `shell-probe.mjs` and `header-probe.mjs`). No separate red capture for these runs exists under `out/sortstring/`: the failing text lives in that proof file. Taken on the uncommitted round tree of 2026-09-19, `main` at `f5bd0e9`.

- **The defects, found by looking at the rendered 通鑑 book (2026-09-18).** Two separate things a reader of `tongjian/ch01-san-jia-fen-jin/index.html` meets: the emptied sort tray printed the English `All sorted.`, and every question label printed 「問題」 and the section rail 「本章目錄」 on a page whose `lang` is `zh-Hans` and whose prose is Simplified. Both live in a stylesheet — `src/styles/components.css` and `tongjian/zj.css` — and the repository's script gate (`test/lexicon.test.js`) reads each page's authored markup and the data files beside it. A word a stylesheet generates is outside that bound, which is exactly why both survived a gate that was working correctly. The English one was reported by the owner; the two Traditional ones were found by the new check, one of them (`本章目錄`) after a census that listed only Latin-lettered strings had missed it.
- Claim (in the test's own header): the `content:` literals of the stylesheets a Simplified page links carry no Traditional-only character; the attributes a reader can be *told* in that page's own markup carry none either; a component word table keyed `zh` or `zh-hans` carries none; every English word a shared stylesheet prints is replaced by the Simplified book that reaches it, through a rule in that book's own stylesheet; and the English a Chinese page can still be shown from shared code is exactly the census the test carries, which fails when a listed string has been translated so that its row is deleted rather than left to rot.
- Bound: it reads files as text. It cannot see a Traditional character that exists only after JavaScript has run, or one in a `::before` whose text comes from a JS string; it does not read a page's visible authored text (that is `test/lexicon.test.js`'s half); it checks one direction of the script rule only, because a Simplified-only character list does not exist in this repository (`traditional-only.txt`'s right-hand column is not one); the word-table scan reads a `zh:`-keyed block and a one-line `*_WORDS` table, and asserts it found the three tables it names so that losing them cannot read as clean; and the census is a tripwire, so a string *added* to shared code tomorrow is not caught. Nothing here renders: a rule that matches an element and loses to a later rule of equal specificity looks identical to this file to a rule that is not there, which is the shape the colour worker measured with `.zj [data-state="wei"]`.
- **Red run 1, the real defect, no mutation needed.** `node --test --experimental-test-isolation=none "test/strings.test.js"`, exit 1, three of four tests green:
  `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: 3 Traditional-only character(s) in the chrome of a Simplified page. The book's rule is that only the 原文 and a quotation marked lang="zh-Hant" are Traditional; this character is in a stylesheet, which test/lexicon.test.js does not read.`
  `    tongjian/zj.css:1396 prints "本章目錄" through ".zj .tb-rail__title::before" on lang="zh-hans" (3 page(s)): 「錄」 is Traditional-only (Simplified: 「录」)`
  `    tongjian/zj.css:1450 prints "問題 " through ".zj tb-check .tb-check__label::before" on lang="zh-hans" (3 page(s)): 「問」 is Traditional-only (Simplified: 「问」)`
  `    tongjian/zj.css:1450 prints "問題 " through ".zj tb-check .tb-check__label::before" on lang="zh-hans" (3 page(s)): 「題」 is Traditional-only (Simplified: 「题」)`
  Fixed: `問題 ` → `问题 ` and `本章目錄` → `本章目录` in `tongjian/zj.css`, and the same word in the comment at `zj.css:1388` that described the pages' `aria-label` (it said 「本章目錄」 while the markup says 「本章目录」). The three chapter pages needed no change: they already carried the Simplified form on both the rail `<nav>` and the opener contents list, which was verified before anything was edited.
- **Red run 2, mutation, one attribute** (`tongjian/ch03-cai-de-lun/index.html:46`, the rail `<nav>`'s `aria-label`, `本章目录` → `本章目錄`). Failure, exit 1, 4 of 5 green, and the diagnostic line that goes with it:
  `✖ a zh-Hans page's own attributes carry no Traditional-only character`
  `ℹ tongjian/ch03-cai-de-lun/index.html lang="zh-hans" — 4 attribute(s) read, 1 Traditional-only character(s)`
  `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: 1 Traditional-only character(s) in an attribute a reader is told, on a page whose lang is Simplified. The rule is that only the 原文 and a run marked lang="zh-Hant" are Traditional; a screen reader on this page reads the modern language and hears the old script.`
  `    tongjian/ch03-cai-de-lun/index.html:46 aria-label="本章目錄": 「錄」 is Traditional-only (Simplified: 「录」)`
  Restored byte for byte: sha256 `E94B274A3C33F7C3227FF609D4617DA7AB5C174C13509F3A9920EE0A67500755` before and after.
- **Red run 3, mutation, one word** (`src/components/sort.js`, the `zh` table's `tray`: `'待归类的条目'` → `'待歸類的條目'`). Failure, exit 1:
  `✖ a component word table that serves a zh-Hans page is Simplified`
  `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: 3 Traditional-only word(s) in a word table the Simplified pages print from. A zh-Hant page has its own key ('zh-hant' in FIGURE_WORDS) and is not read here; a word in 'zh' or 'zh-hans' is printed on a page whose lang is zh-Hans.`
  `    src/components/sort.js:37 — SORT_WORDS.zh says "待歸類的條目": 「歸」 is Traditional-only (Simplified: 「归」)`
  `    src/components/sort.js:37 — SORT_WORDS.zh says "待歸類的條目": 「類」 is Traditional-only (Simplified: 「类」)`
  `    src/components/sort.js:37 — SORT_WORDS.zh says "待歸類的條目": 「條」 is Traditional-only (Simplified: 「条」)`
  Restored byte for byte: sha256 `F9B8A720E569A8C9BB3707DE30A1EA0554DE4E1BB3B854916C286C71C1EB12D2` before and after.
- **Red run 4, mutation, the original defect's class** (`src/styles/components.css`, one rule added: `tb-sort .tb-sort__status::before { content: "All sorted."; }` — a selector the book's tray-scoped override does not cover, which is what makes it the class rather than the instance). Failure, exit 1:
  `✖ every English word a shared stylesheet prints is replaced by the book that reaches it`
  `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: 1 English word(s) a Simplified page can print from a shared stylesheet. A stylesheet cannot know the page's language and a page cannot reach a content: literal, so the replacement is one rule in the book's own sheet.`
  `    src/styles/components.css:643 prints "All sorted." through "tb-sort .tb-sort__status::before", which a tongjian page reaches, and tongjian/zj.css does not replace it. Add:`
  `      .zj tb-sort .tb-sort__status::before { content: "…"; }`
  Restored byte for byte: sha256 `FB7D8D10B5D9C18438F61D682F671D1677307DBD966E2E6B859CEB3A40FC5A0A` before and after; the working tree shows no modification to `src/styles/components.css` once reverted.
- **Which of the file's checks those four red runs prove, and which are owed.** The file as handed over holds six tests. The four red runs prove four of them: the stylesheets' `content:` literals (run 1, on the real defect), the attributes a reader is told (run 2), the component word table (run 3) and the English replacement (run 4). *A word table keeps its two languages in step, key for key* has **no red proof**: it is not in the claim paragraph above, `out/sortstring/gate-proof-text.md` does not mention it, and a scan of every file under `out/` for its failure text finds nothing. Its proof is owed, and nothing here reconstructs it. **That is no longer true as of 2026-09-19: the check is proved, in both directions, and its own sentence is the thing that is wrong.** The `zh` translation of the rail title was deleted from `SHELL_WORDS` and then a misspelt extra key added, each run red at exit 1 — `SHELL_WORDS: 1 key(s) read in en and absent from zh — railTitle` and `SHELL_WORDS: 1 key(s) in zh that en does not have — railTitel; one of the two is a typo` — with `src/shell.js` restored byte for byte to sha256 `11AC61C1…` after each (`out/proofs/owed-three-red-proofs.md`; captures `out/proofs/check1-strings-red.utf8.txt`, `out/proofs/check1b-strings-red.utf8.txt`, `ℹ pass 5 / ℹ fail 1` and `ℹ pass 6 / ℹ fail 0` on the restores). **The assertion is right and the consequence its header and its failure text state is not**: both say a missing key makes the page *print the English word*, because "the lookup merges the language table", and measured, the rail title comes back `""` with **0 child nodes** — `src/shell.js:117` is `SHELL_WORDS[lang] ?? SHELL_WORDS[lang.split('-')[0]] ?? SHELL_WORDS.en`, a whole table or the whole English table and never a key-wise merge with `en`, so only a page's own `textbook.strings` override merges key-wise (`out/proofs/rail-title-probe.mjs`; baseline `"本章目录"`, mutated `""`). The check stays and its sentence is wrong, which is a wording fix in `test/strings.test.js` and not one made here. The red runs' own summaries count four and then five tests, so they were taken while the file was still growing — "three of four" and "4 of 5" are counts of the file at that moment, not of the file as it stands.
- **The census check is proved, and the mutation this bullet used to name could never have proved it.** Its assertion fires when a string leaves `src/shell.js` (or `term.js`/`figure.js`) — files this round did not own, and it was not run then because `npm test`'s shared-code gates were in flight. It has now been run: `|| 'Textbook'` → `|| 'Untitled'` in `src/shell.js` fails, exit 1, `1 of the 3 English string(s) this census records are no longer written the way its row says … src/shell.js: || 'Textbook' — the book name the header would show with no book attribute` (`out/proofs/owed-three-red-proofs.md`, capture `out/proofs/check2b-census-red.utf8.txt`), with the file restored to sha256 `11AC61C1…`. **The one-line mutation the earlier sentence named cannot fire at all**: `'In this chapter'` → `'In this chapter.'` in `SHELL_WORDS.en` was run and gives **exit 0, `ℹ pass 6 / ℹ fail 0`**, because that string is in no row of `SHARED_ENGLISH` — the six shell rows *"were deleted rather than repointed at the table"* when the words moved into `SHELL_WORDS` (`test/strings.test.js:466-470`) — and the three rows left are `|| 'Textbook'`, `'No glossary entry for'` and `This figure could not load` (`:475-479`), each read with `src.includes(row.literal)` (`:486`). So the sentence went stale while the gate was working, which is the shape this file exists to catch: the proof was owed, and the mutation named to take it was aimed at a row that had been deleted. It is still a tripwire on the census rather than a rule about new strings — it fires when a listed string leaves, never when a new one arrives.
- Clean run, same tree, after every restore: `ℹ tests 137 / ℹ pass 137 / ℹ fail 0`, with the attribute scan's own denominators printed per page: `tongjian/ch01… lang="zh-hans" — 4 attribute(s) read, 0 Traditional-only character(s); tongjian/ch02… 4, 0; tongjian/ch03… 4, 0; tongjian/index.html 0, 0; biology/ch01… lang="en" — 12, 0 (not a Simplified surface: counted, not asserted); biology/ch02… 11, 0; biology/ch03… 10, 0; index.html 3, 0; lab/index.html 1, 0`. The Traditional-only text those pages do carry — the 原文, the `<q lang="zh-Hant">` quotations on the book's landing page, the 字詞 glyph column — is excluded from the scan and reported zero hits, which is the exclusion working. The 137 is that run's whole-suite count on the tree of the moment; a later full-suite run of the same round tree records 140 (`out/meta/unit.txt`), so the number moves with the tree and is not this gate's own claim.
- **What it does not prove.** That a word it does not read is right: a rendered check over every page's chrome against its `lang` is what would cover JavaScript-built strings, and `out/sortstring/probe.mjs` in this round is that probe's seed, run by hand because the unit suite must not need a browser. It also proves nothing about pixels: `npm run shot` and a person looking at the frames are what answer those.

# Six gates that were shipped unproved (2026-09-16)

All six below were on `main` at `f5bd0e9`, pushed, and described in `AGENTS.md` as though they had been proved red; none had. They were taken on that revision plus the uncommitted tree of 2026-09-16, with four other workers writing in it (chapter 4's prose and figure registry, the negative-rect fix, the Pages font census, the chapter recipe). Every mutation is one file, restored from a snapshot taken immediately before it and checked by sha256 afterwards; the file's own hash is given each time, and the files other workers held — `tools/shot.js`, `tools/subpath.js`, `tools/devices.js`, `tongjian/*`, `biology/*`, `src/styles/*` — were read and run but never written, which their unchanged hashes state. `npm run unit` as a whole was red throughout for a reason belonging to none of this: `src/figures/registry.js` names `bilayer` and the rest of chapter 4's kinds, whose modules do not exist yet (`bilayer: …\src\figures\bilayer.js does not exist`, written at 18:37 while these runs were going on), so the unit proofs below were taken one file at a time.

## trim: a value that names nothing stops each of the six gates, and does it before the browser (`tools/lib/trim.js`)

- Claim (in the helper's own header): a trimming variable naming nothing that exists stops the run, naming the variable, the value it was given and what it could have been; it never empties the run. Bound, also its own: every name given must be in the list given, and that is all it checks — a run trimmed to one page proves that page and no other, which is the calling gate's header's claim to make.
- Why it exists: `DEVICE_PAGES=ch01` used to filter the page list down to nothing, run zero loads and exit 0 behind `0 load(s) … all clean`. The page ids became book-qualified on 2026-09-12, which is how the trimmed commands quoted earlier in this file went stale without a word.
- **Proved for each of the six tools and for all twelve variables, one run each**, because the tools call the helper separately and one could be miswired. Every run exits 1, prints **nothing at all on stdout**, and names the calling tool's own line. No browser is launched and no `out/` directory is emptied: in all six files the `trim()` calls are at module scope above `rmSync`, `startServer` and `launch`, so the throw lands before any of them.
  - `SHOT_PAGES=ch01` → `tools/shot.js:64` — `Error: SHOT_PAGES=ch01 names no such page: "ch01". A name that matches nothing would run nothing, and a gate that runs nothing must not pass. The pages are: library, biology, biology/ch01, biology/ch02, biology/ch03, biology/ch04, tongjian, tongjian/ch01, tongjian/ch02, tongjian/ch03, today. Page ids carry their book: biology/ch01, not ch01.`
  - `SHOT_VIEWPORTS=390` → `shot.js:63` — `names no such viewport: "390" … The viewports are: phone, tablet, desktop.`
  - `SHOT_THEMES=sepia` → `shot.js:62` — `names no such theme: "sepia" … The themes are: light, dark.`
  - `DEVICE_PAGES=ch01` → `devices.js:179`, the same page list and the same hint; `DEVICE_ONLY=iphone` → `devices.js:178` — `The devices are: phone-small, phone, phone-android, phone-landscape, tablet, tablet-landscape, laptop, desktop, desktop-wide.`; `DEVICE_ENGINES=blink` → `devices.js:180` — `The engines are: chromium, webkit, firefox.`; `DEVICE_THEMES=sepia` → `devices.js:177`.
  - `DRIVE_KINDS=cell3` → `drive.js:25` and `NARROW_KINDS=cell3` → `narrow.js:33`, each listing the twenty-eight registered kinds; `NARROW_THEMES=sepia` → `narrow.js:32`.
  - `SWEEP_KINDS=helix` → `sweep3d.js:34` — `names no such WebGL kind: "helix" … The WebGL kinds are: cell3d, dna3d, water3d, cilium, plantcell3d.`
  - `SITTING_THEMES=sepia` → `sitting.js:49`.
- **The defect itself, reintroduced, because a red guard is not the same statement as "the green was wrong".** With the throw switched off — `if (unknown.length)` made `if (false && unknown.length)`, the pre-fix behaviour and nothing else — `DRIVE_KINDS=cell3 node tools/drive.js` exits **0** with `drive: 0 steps over 0 figures passed; screenshots in out/drive/`. That is the shape the guard exists for: a green summary over a run that did nothing.
